| 5K/10K threshold eligibility (SC-04) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | `tempo_continuous`, `tempo_cruise` and `progressive_tempo` widened to 5K + 10K — threshold work was unreachable for short distances, so the 10K signature declared a `threshold` focus the catalogue could not supply and the engine silently fell back to an aerobic row for the whole build phase. New `INV-PLAN-PHASE-FOCUS-REACHABLE` catches a signature declaring a plan shape the catalogue cannot deliver, per plan rather than per audit. Commit `ea2839e`. |
| 10K race-specific session — `tenk_pace_intervals` (SC-05) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | New catalogue row (4 × 1200 m at goal pace, 2 min jog; peak + taper), mirroring `hm_pace_intervals`. The catalogue had nothing at 10K pace — the nearest was 1000s at 5K pace plus a build session the engine RENAMED "10K-pace progression" on the fly, which made the gap invisible in the product. Would have been dead weight as specced (10K peak prefers `vo2max`; taper force-picked `goal_pace_sharpener` **by id**), so taper selection now ranks eligible `race_specific` rows by `distance_eligibility` size — **most specific wins**, no hardcoded ids. 5K deliberately excluded (race pace ≈ I-pace) and the exclusion recorded in §22 for the board. `INV-PLAN-RACE-SPECIFIC-EXPOSURE` extended to require a real catalogue entry, not a rename. Commit `5b25f16`. |
| Pace inversion surfaced, not silenced (SC-06) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | When an ambitious target pushes goal pace past derived interval pace, sessions labelled VO2max are prescribed SLOWER than sessions labelled race pace while carrying a wider HR band — a plan that cannot be executed as written by both pace and HR. `meta.goal_beyond_measured_fitness` is now derived and `difficulty_band` may not read `comfortable`. **First cross-session invariant** (`INV-PLAN-INTENSITY-ORDERING`): every prior invariant validated one session against its own prescription, which is why nothing caught this — each session was individually defensible and only the pair was incoherent. Board ruling was "reconcile it or surface it", so the inversion is permitted but a plan may not be SILENT about one. Commit `34f423f`. |
| Intensity distribution — session-share ceiling, checked (SC-03, CD-19 + CD-21) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | `INTENSITY_DISTRIBUTION` was declared in config, read by one offline script and **no engine code**, referenced by no invariant — a 4-month §34 gap, which is why nobody could see it was measuring the wrong quantity. Basis corrected to **SESSIONS, PLAN-WIDE, CEILING** (all three words were wrong or unstated): the traced plan's "9.6% vs 25%" was never under-delivery — on the correct basis it delivers exactly 25% in every phase prescribing quality. **The engine was right and the config was wrong.** Ceiling not target, on Seiler's caution that a target invites the engine to close a gap only base phase has room for, pushing a drift-prone population further into Z3 — the failure mode the product exists to prevent. Field renamed `quality_pct` → `max_quality_session_pct`. **Ratified by CD-21 with two amendments** (see next row). New `INV-PLAN-INTENSITY-DISTRIBUTION`. Commits `ae49100`, `23f5c24`. |
| §1 ceiling ratified — maintenance exemption + 100K correction (CD-21) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | Board ruling on the six `max_quality_session_pct` values after the repaired sweep found breaches. First reading ("day-count sensitive") was **wrong**: per-cell analysis splits breaches into **Bucket A** (5K/10K/HM @2d, 50K @3d, 100K @3d — `volume_profile: 'maintenance'` in every case; day count merely triggers §52) and **Bucket B** (100K @4–7d, `build` — a SEVEN-day plan breaches, nothing to do with day count). **Amendment 1:** maintenance-profile plans exempt — a distribution ratio presupposes enough sessions to distribute; at 2 runs/week it is undefined, not violated. Scoped strictly to this ceiling: §7/§2/§9/§45 stay binding. **Amendment 2:** 100K **12% → 15%** — the six values were authored on a MINUTES basis and carried across the basis change unchanged; at 12% this ceiling and **§8 were arithmetically incompatible** (a 6-day 100K build peak runs 2 quality/week = exactly `QUALITY_SESSIONS_PER_WEEK_MAX`), and §1 is the section that yielded. Seiler's dissent recorded against 50K's 15%. Severity restored to `error`. 7 tests, all verified failing before. Commit `23f5c24`. |
| Property sweep repaired — it was validating nothing (SWEEP-VACUOUS-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-08-20 | `scripts/property-validate-plans.ts` printed *"✓ All plans pass invariant validation"* while generating **ZERO plans**, and had for months. `plan_start` was never passed as `generateRulePlan`'s third argument, so the engine derived plan start from **today** while every race date was a fixed 2026 literal; once real time passed those dates every input threw on §44 prep-time and `catch { continue }` swallowed it. **Score on discovery: 37,324,800 attempted, 37,324,800 thrown, 0 validated** — this is the constitutional safety net, and every "sweep → 0 violations" claim before this commit is void. Fixes: plan start passed explicitly + race dates derived from it so it cannot rot again; failures counted and classified; **a 50% minimum-generated floor that FAILS the run**; rewritten as a seeded sample (`SWEEP_N`/`SWEEP_SEED`) + pinned corner cases — 18,056 real plans in ~8s. Missing dimensions added (plan length, long-run day, benchmark, tier, 50K/100K). **A second inert input found in the same file:** the grid passed `blocked_days`, a field `GeneratorInput` does not have (engine reads `days_cannot_train`), so every day-constraint row was inert — invisible to tsc because the sweep types inputs `any`. Renaming it surfaced 2,954 `INV-PLAN-NO-SESSIONS-ON-BLOCKED-DAYS` violations, **all the race**, which is an invariant defect not an engine one (§77: the race ignores `days_cannot_train`) — fixed by excluding `race`. Commits `301d899`, `23f5c24`. |
| CI correctness gate (CI-VERIFY-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-08-20 | `.github/workflows/verify.yml` runs on push to `main`, every PR, and on demand: **typecheck → tests → invariant coverage → archetype matrix → property sweep → build.** Until this landed nothing ran any of these automatically — `.github/workflows/` held only cron jobs and `.githooks/pre-commit` checks style/security, not correctness, so every "verified" claim rested on someone remembering to run the scripts locally (how SWEEP-VACUOUS-01 survived). The plan invariants fail **soft** in production by design, so CI is the only place they bite before a runner sees output. `npm ci` (not `install`) + `tsx` pinned as a devDependency for reproducibility; named scripts `typecheck`/`verify:matrix`/`verify:sweep`/`verify:invariants`/`verify`; ~8s sweep with `concurrency` cancelling superseded runs. |
| Sweep baseline triage (SWEEP-BASELINE-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-08-20 | The 2,306 violations the repaired sweep exposed classified + routed (the deliverable was triage, not mass-fixing). Discriminator: incidence *against volume* — `PEAK-IN-PEAK-PHASE` rises with km (real → VOL-STRUCTURE-01), `LR-PROGRESSION-CAP` falls (real → below), `MIN-SESSION-SIZE` only ≤12 km/wk (degenerate input → INPUT-FLOOR-01), `QUALITY-VARIETY`/`TAPER-VARIETY` 100K-only (catalogue thinness → CAT-ULTRA-THIN-01). Confirmed §1 intensity share holds under volume squeeze (0% breach) because §1 counts sessions, structurally blind to volume loss — so §23 catching the squeeze was not luck. |
| Peak-below-base detrainment fixed (VOL-STRUCTURE-01, §40d) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | 33% of *realistic* plans peaked below their own base phase — the plan detrained the runner silently. Mechanism: a quality session displaces a large easy run with a smaller one and neither remaining slot can absorb the difference (long pinned at `LONG_RUN_CAP_MINUTES`, easy at §9's `long/1.25`); incidence 4%→73% by volume-per-day. Two candidate fixes built and rejected on measurement (relabel-as-maintenance flipped 45% of realistic plans; volume-clamp added 636 new §1 breaches). Shipped: `PEAK_INVERSION_MATERIAL_PCT = 10` serving as both §23's plateau tolerance and §52's fourth maintenance trigger. Structural maintenance now fires on 12% of realistic plans (was 45% ungated), concentrated at high volume-per-day; caps unchanged. All 1080 sweep violations cleared. |
| Long-run progression cap corrected (LR-PROGRESSION-CAP, 981→0) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | Four causes, each traced not reasoned: ① the §12 injury cap ran downstream of the volume curve against the curve's *unadjusted* value so it never compounded (394 violations) — moved into `buildVolumeSequence`, which also fixed taper depth / deload step-down / long-run share all working off volumes the runner never saw; ② §45 differenced two rounded distances against an unrounded cap (199); ③ impossible sweep inputs (5km/wk + 22km long run) — now a fraction of weekly; ④ the cap ran *before* `applyLongRunStepBacks` and didn't know step-backs existed (430, the biggest) — reordering alone would have ratcheted the long run permanently down (the D-21 arithmetic), fixed by extending §45's tolerance to detect a step-back structurally. Marathon long runs still progress 18→26.5km with step-backs intact. |
| Ultra catalogue thinness + row-count variety (CAT-ULTRA-THIN-01) | FREE + PAID (`vert_hike_repeats`) | 2026-08-21 | A 100K build phase had exactly ONE selectable row matching its focus, so the engine reached `progressive_tempo` 7× in 17 quality sessions. New `vert_hike_repeats` (PAID, ultra_specific, effort-governed no-pace — power hiking, the ultra skill the catalogue taught nothing; lower impact than running the vertical) + `threshold_ladder` (FREE, 3-5-8-5-3 at T, closes audit §E.5 rep-length-within-a-set). 100K focus widened to `['ultra_specific','threshold']`. `INV-PLAN-QUALITY-VARIETY-FULL-PLAN` now counts ROWS; `selectCatalogueSession` rewritten stateless-hash → least-used-first with anti-repeat tie-break; `threshold_ladder` gated on `THRESHOLD_LADDER_MIN_WEEKLY_KM = 45` (volume, not an experience label). Row-count residue 2,227→0 across 17,957 plans. Extending `hill_reps` to ultras was rejected (would be dead weight — the SC-05 trap). Also fixed a latent §21 hill-exclusion-in-peak injury bug. |
| Peak-label variety per row structure (LABEL-VARIETY-01) | FREE | 2026-08-21 | On a 100K/marathon time-target plan §22 renamed up to 8 of 17 quality sessions to one string (`"…-pace intervals"`) — training differed, name didn't, and the identical label also tripped §53's cap. The peak override now takes its trailing word from the ROW's own structure (`…-pace ladder`/`sustained`/`reps`), keeping `{dist}-pace` as the lead every §22/§19 check keys on. Peak-only by design (no cross-phase label merge). Words are §19-safe ("sustained"/"reps", never "tempo"). §53 label-count baseline 75→0 across 17,957 plans. |
| Session-size floor by training days (INPUT-FLOOR-01, §52b) | FREE | 2026-08-20 | `INV-PLAN-MIN-SESSION-SIZE` was invisible on either axis alone — it exists only in the interaction (13% below 2km/training-day, 0% at 3+). A 12km/week runner selecting seven days got seven ~1.7km jogs; §52b uses fewer days instead. Floor is the LARGEST session floor `MIN_KM_PER_TRAINING_DAY = 5 = MIN_SESSION_DISTANCE_KM.quality` (a day must be sized for the biggest thing that lands on it). Never below 3 days (§52); does not override life-first §18. A second defect fixed: the invariant measured every quality session against the primary 5km floor when the engine legitimately floors a secondary at 4km — now identifies the secondary structurally. `MIN-SESSION-SIZE` 188→0. |

Fix is an **absolute ceiling not a share** — `VO2MAX_MAIN_SET_MAX_MINS = 20`, decoupled from volume (what a share could never express — why the percentage attempt failed at scale); freed distance returns to easy via §9. 20 is deliberately below elite tolerance and protects the slowest-recovering runner (not yet sex-/fitness-aware — open SLT escalation). Applies to paced flat intervals only (effort-governed hills/hikes excluded). A session is anchored by distance OR duration, and §79/§80 give duration to beginners **and every race ≥ 50 km** — so both `applyLongRunProgressionCap` (producer) and `INV-PLAN-LR-PROGRESSION-CAP` (checker) bailed on `distance_km == null`. **One bug, two copies: the checker could not catch the producer because it shared the defect.** The exemption followed the ANCHOR, not the ability. What was shipping: a 14-week first marathon whose long run went **8.5 km → 26.0 km, +206%**, to 2.9× the runner's lifetime longest, held two weeks at 76% of the week — worse than the +185% incident §45 was written to prevent. Found by the Coaching Board's cold re-review of a generated plan; no check caught it. Measured between the halves as the board required: checker alone **2,271 breaches / 1,568 plans (9.8%)** → both halves **0**. ⚠️ I reintroduced the same producer/consumer split while fixing it (two different km converters) and **parity caught it**; both sides now read `sessionKmSelfPaced` and the duration write-back scales by ratio so no second pace can drift. Downstream declared: maintenance 49.3→48.5, marathon 71.8→69.0, lopsided 9.3%→8.8%. |
| The `avoid` note describes the plan, not the rule (HSR-NOTE-HONESTY-01) | FREE | 2026-09-16 | §110's new `hard_pref_note` fired on `avoid` alone and told **1,615 plans** *"the plan keeps one a week at most"* while delivering **zero** quality — the beginner ceiling zeroes quality before §110's cap has anything to cap, and the note never asked what landed. Measured on a 191-plan HM/marathon grid: the time cap was binding in **ZERO** firings, so **all 191 named the wrong lever**. §40c requires the note to name the lever; naming the wrong one teaches the runner nothing they can act on. | Parity sees `hard_session_relationship` (PARITY-HSR-01) | FREE | 2026-09-16 | `verify-parity` never varied the input (grepped: 0 occurrences), so §110 reported *"IDENTICAL, 5832 cases"* on the very change that rewrote what an `avoid` runner is prescribed. | The plan says when its own target was never reachable (COMPLIANCE-SILENT-3, §40c) | FREE | 2026-09-16 | 3 plans carried a peak below 75% of `peak_km_target` with **no note of any kind** — 5K time goals off 5-12 km/week where the target is 28 km and a 10%/week ramp from 5 km over 11 weeks tops out near 13. **The target was unreachable on day one.** Root cause: **two different shortfalls, one check.** `peak_shortfall_note` only asked whether the plan peaked below the runner's CURRENT VOLUME (§106); nothing asked whether it peaked below its own TARGET (§40c). Every other declaration missed it identically because they measure delivered volume against the internal volume CURVE, which is itself ramp-limited and so reports no shortfall — **the gap was curve-vs-target, which nothing compared.** Gated to time goals (a `finish` runner was never promised a volume). ⚠️ It demoted a better note twice before it was right, both caught by a hash diff: `load_residual_note` yields to it, so a *"week 9 rises 38%, be careful"* SAFETY warning was replaced on golden plans and on 542 parity cases. §106's shortfall makes the residual redundant; §40c's does not. SILENT **3 → 0**. |
| `avoid` is a floor, not a switch (CB-HSR-AVOID-01, §110 + Am.1) | FREE | 2026-09-16 | `hard_session_relationship: 'avoid'` OR an Achilles history set `plannedQuality = 0` for **every week of every plan** — 2,953 non-beginner plans, 18.5% of the sweep, given a race plan with no hard running in it. No board seat could defend zero: Seiler (self-organising recreational runners sit at 10-15% hard; 100/0 is outside any measured population and is a different model, not a cautious 80/20), Willy (the volume curve still ramps, so zeroing intensity swaps intensity risk for monotonous-volume risk — the one that causes bone stress injuries), Sims (close to the worst osteogenic stimulus available, on an option women select more often for cultural reasons), McMillan (*"I avoid hard sessions"* means *"I don't know how"*). **Achilles was a DEFECT, not a ruling** — §21 prescribes substitution and `excludeHillSessions` already performs it; this line deleted the flat session §21 had just substituted, for 756 plans, 400 lines later. **Why it survived: §1 is a CEILING with no floor**, so 0% quality breached nothing and no invariant fired on 2,197 plans — hence `INV-PLAN-QUALITY-NOT-ZERO` as a condition of the ruling. `HARD_AVERSE_QUALITY_PER_WEEK_MAX = 1`, `HARD_AVERSE_QUALITY_DOSE_PCT = 85` (derived: everything below 85 fires a new §52 error on a 3-day HM profile), `QUALITY_FLOOR_MIN_PLAN_WEEKS = 8`, plus `hard_pref_note` for `avoid` (§40c — it fired for `love` ONLY, so 2,197 runners lost every quality session and the plan said nothing). ⚠️ **Am.1 exists because the first sitting's numeric was INERT** — a 1/week cap left `avoid` byte-identical to `neutral` for 72.6% of a 540-cell grid and 100% of intermediates; §96's own defect, caught by `coaching-deviation-scan`, not by review. Two further levers (every-other-build-week; VO2max withheld plan-wide) were built, measured and WITHDRAWN after colliding with §5, §53 and §79 — recorded in §110 Am.1. Also deleted the dead quality logic in `applyInjuryAdjustments` (its `allowQuality` was never destructured, so the Achilles and hip-flexor rules had never fired). Fit-for-purpose **25.6% → 97.7%**. |

Coaching Board cleared it as pure ADR-015 display work (no prescription change either way) — the false-precision concern raised (a warm-up isn't run at one pace) turned out to already exist unaddressed at the top level, since the session's own `distance_km` is itself a one-representative-pace estimate, not a per-segment sum. Log the result in your profile and your paces update for the next block" — stating the opposite of §78 and deleting the instruction the whole recalibration path (ADR-014) depends on, while `meta.recalibration_weeks` went on claiming the week recalibrated. Fixed at the single owner (`lib/plan/enrich.ts`), keyed on `type === 'hard'` — `applyRecalibrationTimeTrial` is the only producer of that type in the codebase. **Root cause worth carrying:** the `hard` typing chosen in §78 so the trial would not count against `QUALITY_SESSIONS_PER_WEEK_MAX` also exempted it from every quality-scoped copy check. `INV-PLAN-COACH-NOTES-MATCH-INTENT` gains a **positive** arm requiring the instruction (not a specific sentence, so rewording still passes). Commit `1742043`. |
| Session zone string derived from the HR target (§84 Amendment 1) | FREE | 2026-09-04 | Coaching Board second sitting, Q1. A live Session Detail card read "Zone 3–4 · threshold / 145–172 bpm" above "Hold 145–158 bpm" — both components faithful, the engine's two fields disagreed (`zone: 'Zone 3–4'` beside `hr_target: qualityHR`, which is `z3Low–z3Top`, Zone 3 ONLY). **§84 itself was the cause:** its Config paragraph asserted the engine wrote the two "consistently in every branch", true for VO2 and ASSUMED for threshold, then rebuilt the header on `session.zone` on the strength of it; before §84 the header read `hr_target` and the two agreed. Fixed at the cause — `zones.qualityZone`/`intervalsZone` are authored in the same expression as the HR strings so they cannot drift. **No prescribed HR changed** (41 golden lines, all "Zone 3–4" → "Zone 3"). Invariant extended to assert the zone band EQUALS `hr_target` for range targets, reading HR from `plan.meta` not the raw input (§50's guard can reject a sub-estimate max — a first cut read input and fired 11,556 times). Ceilings and the `Zone 2–3` long run deliberately out of scope → ZONE-BAND-01/02. Commit `3b9280a`. |
| Quality-session distance priced segment by segment (§8 Amendment) | FREE | 2026-09-04 | Coaching Board second sitting, Q2 — amends the 2026-09-03 structure-driven-sizing ruling. `effectiveDistKm` divided the whole session duration by WORK pace, pricing a 15-min warm-up and 4.5-min cool-down at threshold/VO2max pace: a card read 10 km while its own steps summed to 8.4. Overstatement 5K 25.7%, 10K 23.5%, HM 11.4%, MAR 19.1%; `weekly_km` sums these so the inflation landed on the hard component specifically. Now priced per segment, with duration derived independently from the structure. **Freed distance flows to easy via §9's re-derivation** (sums actual placed distances) — measured mean weekly change **+0.10%**, total 779 → 786 km, so volume is preserved rather than cut, per the board's unanimous amendment. **Two self-inflicted bugs caught by tests, not reasoning:** duration initially followed distance down (46 → 41 min, reintroducing §40b Am 2's defect one field over), and the floor checks still used the old pricing — `pacedRepPlan`'s loop divided by work pace and `tempo_continuous` had NO floor protection, producing a 4.5 km session against a 5 km floor. `segmentPricedDistance()` is now the single owner of that arithmetic. `continuousThresholdExpectedMainMins` reads the session's `derived_set` rather than the config table, since floor growth is runner-specific. |
| ENRICH-SERVER-SAVE-01 — the server persists the enriched plan | TIER-DIVERGENT (trial/paid) | 2026-09-04 | **Not in backlog — emergent.** ENRICH-SAVE-01 rightly removed a 15s blocking wait (the enricher takes 28–35s), saving the plan immediately and writing the enriched copy "as a follow-up" — but that follow-up was owned entirely by the CLIENT. So the design told the runner not to wait and then silently cost them the paid voice layer when they didn't: tap "Use this plan", lock the phone, `meta.enrichment` stuck on `pending` for ever. `/api/generate-plan` only ever READ the `plans` table; nothing server-side persisted anything. Observed on a real trial plan saved 5s after generation. The route now writes the enriched plan before closing the stream, guarded by `shouldServerPersist` so it can only overwrite the plan it just produced — the row must exist AND its `meta.generated_at` must match this generation, with a missing/non-string timestamp declining rather than guessing (12 of 17 stored plans predate the field). Client keeps its follow-up write as the fast path; UX unchanged. Race degrades correctly both ways. New ops kinds `plan_enrich_server_saved` / `_save_failed` — the success case recorded deliberately, since a backstop nobody can see firing is one nobody trusts. **Follow-up the same day (`77cc68a`): the chain was moved OUT of `ReadableStream.start()` and under `waitUntil`.** It had been built after a 28–35s await *inside* the stream body — the one place it could not do its job, since a runner locking their phone disconnects the client and can take the stream and the function down before the write lands. The tell was that this repo already used `waitUntil` in `app/api/health/ingest/route.ts` for exactly this class of work while `generate-plan` used neither it nor `maxDuration`. The chain is now wrapped so it cannot reject (a rejected promise handed to `waitUntil` is an unhandled rejection on the platform's side), and the `waitUntil` call itself is guarded so a missing request context degrades to stream-lifetime behaviour rather than 500-ing generation. **Still unverified in production** — the proof is a `plan_enrich_server_saved` event from a generation where the app is closed immediately. |
| ENRICH-PARTIAL-01 — one bad week no longer discards the whole plan's AI voice | TIER-DIVERGENT (trial/paid) | 2026-09-04 | **Not in backlog — emergent.** Found by checking a plan the founder generated to verify the day's other fixes. Diagnosed from a real trial plan that generated with `enrichment: "failed_invalid_copy"`. `INV-PLAN-COPY-MATCHES-SESSIONS` (§27) rejects week copy promising what the week does not contain; the check is right, the CONSEQUENCE was not — one offending word on one week discarded enriched copy for **every** week. Two violations on two of the five weeks carrying no intensity cost the runner voice on all fourteen. **Three causes, all fixed:** (1) the enrich prompt never stated the §27 constraint, offered *"Build — first quality session"* as an exemplar label, and told the model to use *session-type* language — the exact vocabulary the invariant polices; (2) the model had to infer which weeks were safe, so each week now carries an explicit `has_intensity`, computed with the SAME predicate the invariant uses so prompt and check cannot disagree; (3) the route now reverts only the OFFENDING WEEKS (`lib/plan/enrichPartialRevert.ts`), falling back to a full revert only when a violation cannot be attributed to a week or survives the per-week repair — re-validated, never assumed. New `applied_partial` enrichment status. Ops event now records `outcome`, `reverted_weeks`, `weeks` and `messages`; it previously recorded codes alone, which made the diagnosis a manual reconstruction. **Follow-up the same day, from the first live generation after the fix:** the prompt was told a single conflated `has_intensity` that counted the §78 time trial (typed `hard`) as intensity, but §27's claim table asks TWO questions — `/sharpen/` requires a QUALITY session specifically, because a measurement is not something you sharpen on. The model was told a deload week "had intensity", wrote "Build — recovery and sharpening", and the invariant correctly rejected it, costing that one week its voice. Both predicates now come from a single owner, `lib/plan/weekIntensityFlags.ts`, which the invariant and the prompt both derive from, and the prompt splits the policed words into the three groups §27 actually enforces. **This was the `checker reads a different source from the producer` class committed inside the fix meant to prevent it** — hence the shared owner and a test that pins the prompt's wording to the claim table. **Third iteration, same afternoon:** the next generation came back `applied_partial` on weeks 11 and 12 — the OVERLOAD arm this time ("Copy implies overload but weekly_km 30km <= prior non-deload 43km"), i.e. the model claiming a build on TAPER and RACE weeks. The prompt did state that rule, but as a RULE requiring it to compare `weekly_km` across weeks it is not tracking. Replaced with an `is_overload_week` flag from the same shared owner, and the invariant's own overload arm now routes through it too. **The lesson across all three iterations is one thing: give the model the ANSWER, not the rule.** A flag is not a smaller ask than a rule — it is a different one. **Structural, not unlucky:** base phase is all-easy by design (§4/§5), so exposure is highest for beginners and finish-goal plans — least likely to know the app should sound different, most likely to be on trial. Verified by replaying the real failure against the stored plan: 0 of 14 weeks kept voice, now 11 of 14 (12 in production, where `applyEnrichment` already protects the §78 time trial). |
| Input-coverage gate on the property sweep | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-04 | Proposal #4 of the 2026-09-03 test-coverage set. Asserts the SHAPE OF THE GRID before any plan generates; field names are **parsed from the declaration**, never listed, because a hand list is the defect itself. `assertParsedShape` throws if the parser returns an implausible list, so a broken parser cannot make the gate pass vacuously; absence counts as a distinct value (the §79 gap in one rule); a stale exemption naming a removed field is surfaced, not ignored. **Found 7 unset fields on the first run** — 2 real engine paths now swept (`weeks_at_current_volume`, and `foundation_decision`, whose compose call had HARDCODED `'add'` so ADR-020's skip/start_now path was unreachable), 5 exempted with reasons traced to each field's only reader. Commit `d5355c3`. |
| FRESH-FLOOR-01 — a foundation week smaller than one session's floor | FREE | 2026-09-04 | Found by the input-coverage gate on its first run: 1,426 `INV-PLAN-MIN-SESSION-SIZE` violations across 660 of 16,141 plans, unreachable since M-02 declared `weeks_at_current_volume`. §29's fresh-from-layoff path treats `current_weekly_km` as aspirational and starts at 0.7 of it; at 5 km/week that is a 3.5 km week, §52b day-fitting correctly reduces it to ONE run, and that run IS the week — 3.5 km against a 4 km easy floor. §52b had nothing left to give (it reduces days; one is the minimum), so D-21 applies: a floor a valid input cannot satisfy is a defect in the code enforcing it. | MAX-WEEKEND-MINS-01 — a declared input field with no implementation, removed | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-04 | `max_weekend_mins` sat on `GeneratorInput` with **zero readers** in `lib/` or `app/`, no sender (the wizard captures only the weekday cap), no entry in `docs/contracts/api/generate-plan.md`, and no real user had ever supplied it — while `coaching-rules.md` claimed the engine "respects" it. | VO2max dose reachability recorded as a limit, not fixed (EG-01) | FREE (doctrine) | 2026-09-04 | Coaching Board second sitting — **CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT, and the amendment is that there is no code change.** `intervals_long` (1000 m reps) reaches its fitness×phase work target in 304/782 = 39% of placements vs `intervals_classic` (3 min reps) at 647/647 = 100%; median 13.7 min against a 15–18 target. Cause: at 4.56 min/km a kilometre rep is 4.56 min, so 3 reps = 13.7 and 4 = 18.2, over the hard ceiling — two admissible counts, 33% apart. **Ruled not a defect:** the ceiling is what stops a distance-anchored rep costing a 6:00/km runner 24 min at Z4–5, 13.7 is inside the safety-relevant band, and the shortfall is worst mid-pace so it is quantisation, not bias. What is lost is the progression, not the session. Both remedies rejected — re-specifying rep length would delete the session (SC-08: rep length is the stimulus identity), and a selection preference measured as unimplementable without violating §53. §8 amendment records the limit explicitly; **not mechanically checkable** (ADR-017 §4). |

| Quality in calendar week 2 for a demonstrated runner (§97, CB-ONSET-03) | FREE | 2026-09-07 | For a §89-gated runner, surplus calendar weeks are no longer filled with a §57 foundation block — **the plan extends to the signature's `max_weeks` instead**, so those weeks are validated and periodised. The on-ramp floor drops to `MIN_ONRAMP_WEEKS_GATED` (1) with VO2max/hills withheld meanwhile (Willy's condition of approval). **Delivered: quality in calendar week 2, first VO2max in week 4.** The argument that carried it: §76's *"surplus delays the start"* does not create rest — the runner trains those weeks either way — it creates training OUTSIDE the periodisation arc, carved out of five invariants, which §57's own text calls *"habit and routine, not adaptation"*. `max_weeks` was dead config (PLANLEN-DUP-01); this honours a bound §17 already declared. **The defect it surfaced, four steps from its cause:** the re-entry window covered the week holding build's single VO2max slot, the slot was read off the rotation's modulo and so was SPENT rather than deferred, build ended with zero VO2max, the first exposure fell into peak past §5's deadline, and that fired V2's swap safety net — which carries its own documented goal-pace defect. Fixed by stating the slot **positively** (build carries one exposure at the first eligible index at or after the natural one). *A scarce resource should be placed, not discovered.* **§1 yielded nothing:** the first build hit 18.4% against the 18% MARATHON ceiling, so §97 yielded twice — `MAX_ONSET_PLAN_EXTENSION_WEEKS` (2, *renamed `MAX_PLAN_EXTENSION_WEEKS` on 2026-09-16*) and `ONSET_SHORT_ONRAMP_DISTANCES` (5K/10K/HM). The distance scope is not parochial: §1's ceiling descends with distance while a shorter base raises the quality share, so the short on-ramp is affordable exactly where the ceiling is loosest, and a marathon's base carries the long-run progression (Willy). **Recorded, not chased:** delivered onset improves on six of seven weeks-to-race rows and regresses on one (16 weeks, 3 → 4) because the extension moves `plan_start` into §57's auto band. |
| `overdo` is a brake, not a preference (§96, CB-HSR-01) | FREE | 2026-09-07 | `hard_session_relationship: 'overdo'` — *"I overdo it. Rein me in."* — was **byte-identical to `neutral` in every cell** of training_age × distance × injury. Four wizard options, one of which could never change anything for any runner, ever. **The structural gap is §35's:** it builds a three-tier ladder UPWARD (floor → target → stretch, selected by `love`) and says the tiering *"will eventually apply to weekly volume, quality session frequency, and goal-pace exposure"* — it never built the rung going the other way. **§79 is the decisive argument:** self-report is already held to be asymmetric — trusted more pointing toward caution than toward more work — and `overdo` is a caution signal weighted at zero. Two effects: it **vetoes §89/§91 early quality onset** however many readiness signals pass (a runner who says they cannot self-regulate load is the last who should reach intensity two weeks sooner — Willy/McMillan), and it puts §24c's existing Z2-ceiling cue on **every long run** at every distance and phase rather than build-phase 5K/10K only (reusing existing copy, once per week on the session where Z3 drift is worst — a cue you experience, not a rule you study). **Measured cost, stated because the first draft of §96 claimed neutrality and the tests caught it:** the veto lengthens base, so quality goes 10 → 6 and volume 482 → 457 km — landing **exactly on the plan a runner who never passed the gate gets**. The brake declines to accelerate; it does not cut below baseline. Deliberately NOT done: no quality suppression (that is `avoid`, a different ask), no deload change (§3/§87), no ramp tightening (§94 governs that for everyone). `INV-PLAN-OVERDO-BRAKE` (error), falsification-tested. **Two findings recorded open:** §24b's segmented long run is `type: 'easy'` yet 50% at MP/HM pace, so §1 excludes it (17.9% counted vs 21.4% if counted, ceiling 25% — nothing breaches today, but §35 anticipates adding more); and `love` is gated on `training_age: '5yr+'` with nothing telling the runner the answer is conditional (copy, routed to brand). |

The test carries a falsification case and its substring matching is deliberately biased toward passing (a key named in a comment counts), so it can miss an orphan but never falsely accuse one. |
| Quality onset survives the foundation block (§91/§92, CB-ONSET-02) | FREE | 2026-09-07 | **§89 did not deliver what §89 says it delivers.** It shortened base so quality starts ~2 weeks sooner — inside `plan.weeks`. §76 then delays the start (a 10K caps at `idealWeeks` 12, surplus is delayed not truncated), ADR-020 fills the delay with all-easy §57 foundation weeks, and `computePhases` never saw them. Measured delivered onset by weeks-to-race: **4 / 5 / 6 / 6** at 12/13/14/15 weeks vs **3** for an identical runner with no block — **non-monotonic**, so entering a race EARLIER made the plan more conservative, and **62% of swept plans (9,905/16,035) carry a block**. Two changes, both gate-scoped: foundation weeks are CREDITED against the base floor (they are the same all-easy running at current volume — Seiler's floor is a physiological on-ramp, not an administrative one), and early-onset base is capped in WEEKS (`EARLY_ONSET_BASE_MAX_WEEKS`) as well as percent, because a fraction re-grew the base on longer plans. Base may reach ZERO — two foundation weeks ARE the two-week floor. `plannedFoundationWeeks()` is the single owner of the count, called by both `generateRulePlan` (to size base) and `composePlanWithFoundation` (to build the block) — they previously derived it independently and disagreed by a week whenever a plan was generated on a Monday. Stamped as `meta.foundation_weeks_planned` because `validatePlan` runs on both the bare and composed plan and reading the array would answer the same question two ways. `INV-PLAN-ONRAMP-FLOOR` replaces the base-only arm of `-EARLY-ONSET-GATED`, **which tested `baseWeeks === 1` exactly and so let a ZERO-week base pass silently** — an equality test on a floor is a hole. **§92:** §57's stride ban lifted for gated runners only (neuromuscular, fully recovered, no Z3 minutes, not `quality`, session type unchanged so `INV-PLAN-FOUNDATION-BLOCK` still holds) — a FEEL fix, recorded as such. **REFUSED and recorded in §91:** quality inside the foundation block (§57/CB-1 pre-empted it three days earlier) and dropping the floor (Seiler's condition on §89). Falsification-tested both ways. |
| Peak rehearses the race (§93, CB-SPEC-01) | FREE | 2026-09-07 | **§5's `SPECIFICITY_BY_PHASE` had never been read by engine code** — it appears in `generationConfig.ts`, three documents and one `keyof` type alias, and nothing computed it. So nobody could see that 10K peak delivered **0% specific against a declared 60%**, the exact inverse, while HM delivered 5/5. The §1/CD-19 failure verbatim, one section over. Cause: `preferredQualityCategory` opened its peak branch with an unconditional `if (5K || 10K) return 'vo2max'` — the leftover CD-16 named (*"peak-only was a leftover from the superseded assumption that VO2max was the specific work for a 10K"*) and did not remove. Once §91 grew peak to five weeks the same unbounded return produced **five consecutive VO2max sessions for a 44-year-old** with zero race-pace before the taper. Peak VO2max now capped at `min(PEAK_MAX_VO2MAX_SESSIONS, ceil(peakWeeks/2))` — honours CD-16's "peak's two" on a long peak without letting a short peak run all-general; remainder is race-specific (time goal) or threshold (finish goal, CD-2/§80). 5K untouched (§22/SC-05 — race pace ≈ I-pace). Two sibling defects fixed with it: the second-slot `altCategory` map was **not exhaustive** and fell through to the primary's own category, so a race-specific primary filled BOTH slots from one category — and 10K owns ONE such row, shipping the identical session twice in a week (latent while only HM reached that branch); and §22's ratio arm still classified VO2max **by label**, so `hill_reps` and `intervals_rolling` inflated its denominator — SC-09 added `isVo2maxSession` for exactly this and fixed only §22's sibling arm. `INV-PLAN-PEAK-SPECIFICITY` (warn) finally exercises §5. **Recorded, not fixed:** `peak_includes_race_pace` and `peak_includes_mp_long_runs` in `planSignatures.ts` are read by no engine code either. |

The mechanism is a **safety rule creating the spike**: `V1-volume-quality-split` (Willy's gate on CD-16) holds a week flat when the first VO2max lands — correct — but the next week steps up from the CURVE value, so the trim hands its whole deficit forward. Founder's live 10K: curve 33→37→40 (+8%, legal), trim held W2 at 33, runner ran **33/33/40 = +23%** against a chronic load of 33; on the pre-§91 shape the same mechanism produced **+48%**. Measured 525-plan grid **before vs after §91/§93: 24.4%→17.0% of plans, worst week rise 76%→63%** — pre-existing and *reduced* by this session's rulings, not introduced. Two scopes forced by measurement (the first draft fired on **44.4%**, worst 114%, almost all noise): binds only above `current_weekly_km` (§2 guards load the body has not ADAPTED to — a runner on 20 km/wk given 13.5→16.5→18 is below their own load throughout), and the whole week AND its non-long-run portion must both breach (the §52-exempt long run is race-anchored, so the trimable remainder swings +114% for one extra km). **Deliberately does NOT change the producer** — re-anchoring the next week would lower delivered peak, the ceiling §79/§89 protect, and the board's own precedent (a healthy bounceback cap built, measured and rejected 2026-09-06) requires measurement first. Count was never the rule, and treating it as one is what let the wizard drift. Promoted from three bare chips to CardSelect: it is the highest-stakes answer in the wizard (the demonstrated-readiness signal §89/§91 gate on, so a careless tap moves the first quality session two weeks) and it shipped directly before `your-level`, the same kind of question with full cards. Across 504 plan shapes: 0 boundary deloads, 0 over-long loading blocks, 0 back-to-back deloads, **0 reductions in recovery**. **Gotchas:** the mechanism is RE-ANCHORING, not a ±1 shift — shifting one week always lengthens the block on the other side, so the board's own rules 1 and 3 rejected both directions and the first implementation silently moved nothing. A backward normalisation pass then restores even spacing ({3,5,8} → {2,5,8}), because the greedy forward pass otherwise leaves two deloads one week apart. `INV-PLAN-DELOAD-PLACEMENT` is scoped to base/build by ALLOWLIST — the exclusion form counted `foundation`/`maintenance_*` weeks (ADR-020, week numbers ≤ 0) and failed two real stored plans. Recovery count is checked as a DIRECTION (may rise, never fall), not equality: the raw cadence was under-delivering in ~10% of shapes. |

| Delivered volume tracks the ceiling (DELOAD-INVERSION-01 / §90) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-06 | Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT (Willy-led). Three levers: **(1)** a deload = `min(existing, 70% × POST-CAP prior week)`, not 70% of the uncapped curve — curve inversions **12.8%→0%**, and deloads keep their day **frequency**; **(2)** an injury (knee/shin) peak week yields its 2nd quality session to §12 (§8 defers) so the delivered cap is achievable; **(3)** easy runs trim/drop to the ceiling while the **race-anchored long run is never trimmed** (§52 owns it). Plus one maintenance trigger: injury + beginner volume + ultra (`distKm>43`) → `maintenance` not an unsafe ramp. **Injury-scoped** — healthy delivered divergence stays §52's lopsided→maintenance (Hutchinson, firm). **Honest residual:** not fully closed — floor-dominated low-volume injury plans and the §52 long run still exceed the cap at delivery, so all three enforcing invariants (`INV-PLAN-DELOAD-IS-A-REDUCTION`, **new** `INV-PLAN-INJURY-CAP-DELIVERED`, `INV-PLAN-BOUNCEBACK-BOUNDED`) are `warn`, declared-and-exercised (§34), promoting to `error` only when long-run placement is itself reconciled. No new coaching numeric (reuses `RECOVERY_WEEK_VOLUME_PCT`, `INJURY_WEEKLY_INCREASE_CAP_PCT`). Verified: 981 tests, sweep 16k / 0 NEW violations, matrix 17/17, `deloadInversion.test.ts` (7). | §89 onset yields to §1 — the yield ladder (§98 / CB-ONSET-YIELD-01 / INTENSITY-3DAY-01) | FREE | 2026-09-10 | §89's experience-gated early quality onset was breaching §1's distance ceiling **on its own**: measured 34 of 212 gated plans over ceiling on a 240-plan grid, worst **HM@3d 29.5% vs 20%**; the same cells with the gate closed gave **zero**. **The defect was filed against §91's foundation credit and that was wrong** — isolation on 120 comparable cells gave 29 breaches gate-open / 0 gate-closed, and **11 of the 29 carried no foundation block at all**; a remedy aimed at the credit took 34 → 31. §97's distance + headroom gates could not reach it: they set the base **cap**, and §91's credit subtracts from the cap, so base hit 0 in all twelve distance × day cells whatever the gate ruled. `generateRulePlan` is now a **ladder** over `buildRulePlanOnce` (the former monolith): walk base back one week at a time until §1 is satisfied, **bounded by the effective on-ramp an ungated runner would get** (§91: base + `foundation_weeks_planned`), falling through to the genuinely ungated plan if no rung complies. A ladder rather than a numeric because **three threshold levers died** — breaches occur at `ceiling_fraction × days_available` of **0.60 and 1.25**, so no proxy of that shape separates them; this measures the real §1 share instead of predicting it, so the breach is impossible by construction. **The bound is load-bearing**: unbounded, it cleared all 34 and introduced a regression where a runner waited LONGER than if they had never demonstrated readiness (§91's non-monotonic onset). Result: **178 plans untouched at full §89 benefit (84%)**, 33 corrected (rungs 1/2/3 = 13/16/4), 1 fell through, **0 breaches, 0 worse than ungated**, mean cost +1.71 on-ramp weeks. Artifacts: §98 · `GENERATION_CONFIG.ONSET_YIELD_MAX_RUNGS` (4 — a safety stop, not a tuning knob) · `INV-PLAN-ONSET-YIELD-BOUNDED` reading the stamped `meta.onset_yield`. Sweep baseline `INV-PLAN-INTENSITY-DISTRIBUTION` **2 → 0, debt paid**. `onsetYieldLadder.test.ts` is deliberately **deterministic** (the random grid is what let INTENSITY-3DAY-01 ship) and **falsification-tested**: 12 of 13 go red with the ladder disabled. Verified `npm run verify` exit 0 — 1106 tests / 134 files, 89 invariants, matrix green, sweep 16,038 plans / 0 violations; `next build` exit 0. **Runner-facing half shipped the same day (ONSET-YIELD-NOTE-01):** `onsetYieldNote()` joins the `planRationaleNotes()` family under the label **Quality timing** — *"Quality starts later here than your training history alone would allow — across the whole plan, more hard sessions would tip too much of it out of easy."* ONE string covers both outcomes (trimmed, and fell-through) so the copy cannot drift from what the engine did. Fires only on the 34 stamped plans; the 178 untouched say nothing (Wood). **It also caught a live overclaim §98 had just introduced**: `levelFitNote`'s *"starts earlier than a novice plan"* becomes FALSE when the ladder trims to a tie (`effective === bound`), so it is now suppressed in that case — measured, the note renders on all 34 stamped plans with 0 cut by the 3-note cap. Falsification-tested both ways. |

| LONGEST-RUN-GATE-01 — §113 long-run readiness, the third ungoverned refusal | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | **Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT.** 🔴 **The OPPOSITE ruling to §111 on substance, the SAME on governance** — and the distinction is the point. §111's gate was **non-monotonic** (refused 15 km/wk at +135%, permitted 20 at +160%) so its threshold was wrong; this one **is** monotonic and tracks what it protects against, so the threshold is **right**. Ruling it like §111 for consistency would have destroyed a correct threshold. **Measured:** longest 5 km → week 1 **+7%**; 4 km → +21%; 2 km → **+142%**. **Why exactly that number:** §45's `WEEK_1_2_LONG_RUN_CAP_MULTIPLIER` (1.10) works down to 5, then `MIN_SESSION_DISTANCE_KM.long` is applied **after** it and silently wins — the engine cannot honour its own cap below its own floor. What was wrong: a hardcoded `5` in an API route, ungoverned, invisible to `configPrincipleSync`/`coaching-guard`, a bare string where §44 requires alternatives, and a **second copy of a number that already had an owner**. `minLongestRunKm()` reads the floor; **the route now holds no coaching number at all**. Thrown as `LongRunReadinessError` mirroring §44/§52/§111. **A missing longest run PASSES** — absence is not shortness. ⚠️ **First measurement was WRONG** (divided duration by a hardcoded 7 min/km on a duration-anchored cohort) and nearly reached the board. ⚠️ **Blast radius:** two fixtures used `longest_recent_run_km: 4`, now unbuildable — raised with the reason recorded, not silently. §113 refuses **0 of 35,952 grid rows** (their minimum longest run is 8, above the floor) but **2,634 in the property sweep** — a corpus limitation, not a dead rule; filed as `GRID-SUBFLOOR-01`. 12 tests, 3 mutations killed. |

Now derived through `formatDuration`; `No limit` stays a **word**. (2) 🔴 The selected chip was stored as its **LABEL**, in React state AND in the saved `zona_wizard_draft`, matched back by `find(c => c.label === maxWeekdayChip)` — so renaming a label makes an in-flight draft match nothing, `?.value` yields `undefined`, and **the runner's weekday cap silently becomes "No limit"**, changing the plan they get. Chips now carry a stable `key` and derive the label: **identity and display are different things.** ⚠️ **Legacy drafts are shimmed** (`normaliseWeekdayChip`) — without it the fix causes the very defect it prevents for anyone mid-wizard at deploy. | FIRSTRUN-MISSED-01 (part 2) — §112, a "Too tired" skip counts as reported cost | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | **Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT.** 🔴 **The conflict scan stopped the sitting before it started: `§R20-T4` DOES NOT EXIST.** `constants.ts:89` cited it for a year; the constitution's only `R20` is an unrelated tier gate — while **§70's reframe risk gate and the recalibration trigger both DEPEND on the mechanism** without defining it. The §92 class inverted (there a principle named a missing invariant; here code named a missing principle). ⚠️ **NOT a file-path bypass** like §106/§111 — CLAUDE.md sanctions `lib/coaching/constants.ts` for load thresholds; the file was right and only the principle was missing, and calling it the same would have sent the remedy to the wrong layer. **Substance:** a runner who logs `Heavy` COMPLETED the session; one who reports `'Too tired'` could not begin it (Willy: the stronger signal; Sims: a low-energy-availability presentation the old design could never observe). **Measured, n stated: 1 runner shows `Injury / illness → Too tired ×3` and the plan never softened; `FATIGUE_ACCUMULATION` has NEVER fired in production.** n=1/3 events is a signal not a rate, so the threshold stays at 3. **McMillan's dissent enforced in code** — `FATIGUE_WINDOW_REQUIRES_LOGGED_SESSION`: a skip may contribute but may not fill the window alone. 🔴 **And the trigger was blind for a reason neither I nor the SLT had found:** the route fetched the window with `.eq('status','complete')`, so a skipped session was **never in it, from the beginning** — part 1 fixed the storage and left the signal exactly as unreachable. Widened to `.in('status',['complete','skipped'])` with the bare-stub protection **preserved** as an `.or()` over both columns. `fatigueAccumulation.ts` is the single owner (window assembled in the route, judged by an inline `.every()` — two halves of one rule in two files). **Enforced by a NAMED TEST, not an invariant**, because `validatePlan` sees a plan object and this runs at coaching time against `session_completions`: no `Plan => Violation[]` can reach it by construction. Registered in `principleCoverage` and added to the liveness harness — **5 mutations, all killed**. |

| LR-SHORTFALL-CAUSE-01 — the shortfall note named the wrong lever | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | **Coaching Board, §80 Amendment 1.** §80's long-run shortfall note offers two causes; **it named the time cap 0 times in 5,264 firings** and told every runner *"your weekly volume is what limits it"* — while **71.0% of them sat 2–3 minutes beneath that very cap**. The producer carried a comment from the §110 sitting claiming it *"names whichever one is actually binding"*; claim and computation had never been reconciled. **Cause was structural:** `LONG_RUN_CAP_MINUTES` is applied on the **kilometre** axis (`absCapMins / paceMinPerKm`) and the distance is then rounded, so a capped long run lands just under its ceiling and rarely satisfied the `+1` predicate. **The line is measured:** gap histogram 2 min × 2,420 · 3 min × 1,316 · **nothing at 4–5** · tail from 6 — two populations, and the empty band means 3 sits in a flat region. Added `LONG_RUN_AT_CAP_TOLERANCE_MINS` (3) and `LONG_RUN_SHORTFALL_MATERIAL_PCT` (5) — the note had **no materiality floor at all** and fired at a 2-minute shortfall (1.7% of the floor) attached to *"expect the last stretch of race day to be new territory"*; §40c: *"notes that fire on noise get ignored"*. **The cap branch now names no lever, on purpose** (McMillan): when the ceiling binds there is none, and §40 settled that the caps do not move. Willy: telling a marathoner two minutes under a 210-minute ceiling to look at their weekly volume is an injury vector served as advice. **After: 3,736 name the ceiling, 1,392 name volume, 0 volume-blaming notes within 5 min of the cap, 136 silenced as noise.** `INV-PLAN-LR-SHORTFALL-CAUSE` reads the NOTE TEXT, not the producer's predicate, and is proven wakeable by a new liveness mutation. ⚠️ **The board's first framing said the branch was "structurally dead" and that was TOO STRONG** — `noteNamesBindingLever.test.ts` builds a persona that does reach the cap. Corrected in all four artifacts: zero in the corpus is not "cannot fire". **`verify:parity` with notes stripped on both sides: IDENTICAL, 5,940 cases** — this changed what the plan SAYS, never what it prescribes. |
| ONBOARD-EXIT-01 — a way out of onboarding, and an optional field that says so | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | **Two first-run fixes, SLT-reviewed 2026-09-18.** (1) **Sign-out was unreachable during onboarding.** The gate sequence (wizard → Orientation → Connect Runs → Push) renders in front of the Me screen and hides the bottom nav, and the wizard's back button is suppressed on step 0, so a user on the wrong account was trapped; the only escape was backing out to an empty Today, then Me, then the **"Careful Now"** section that also holds Delete Account. New `components/shared/SignOutLink.tsx` is the single owner, on all four screens. ⚠️ **ConnectRunsScreen already had a hand-rolled copy and it had DRIFTED** — it never called `clearWidgetState()`, so signing out there left the previous account's race countdown on the home-screen widget, and a user at that screen HAS a plan. Wood's binding constraint at the review: do **not** reuse the `ui-patterns.md` "Careful Now" destructive grouping here — correct on Me, an ejector seat on a welcome screen. Shown in the wizard only when `isOnboarding`, since a runner regenerating a plan arrived from Me with a working back button. Tap target raised 40px → 44px (iOS HIG). (2) **The optional first-name field now says it is optional.** It was already optional in code (no `required`, and `...(fullName ? {data} : {})`) but rendered as the first of three identical boxes above two that are required. **The founder asked to delete it; the board declined** — it feeds six prompt builders, the trial emails and the avatar initials, and removal would part-revert PROFILE-NAME-01 shipped the day before. Naming it optional delivers the same slickness and keeps the name. Placeholder, not a label: the form is placeholder-only for email and password. Verified on a 375px preview — renders `First name (optional)`, accessible name `First name, optional`. **`lib/auth/signOut.ts` owns the SEQUENCE** (clear the App-Group widget store, end the session, hard-navigate to `/auth/login`) while the two affordances keep their own presentation, so the Me-screen button and the onboarding link cannot drift again; `signOutOwner.test.ts` walks `app/` and `components/` and fails the build on any hand-rolled `auth.signOut()` outside the owner or its two stated exemptions. ✅ **`/onboarding-preview`** is the dev-only fixture page for these four auth-gated screens, and **it earned its keep immediately**: seeing the real component in the real footers showed that on Connect Runs the link sat directly under that screen's **"Not now →"** skip as a near-identical muted pair (fixed with 8px separation, not a divider, which is banned), and that the `disabled` state changed only the cursor and so looked enabled (fixed with opacity). **Neither was reachable by any test.** ⚠️ **Verified by EXECUTION, not by rendering** (third pass, after two challenges): `lib/auth/signOut.test.ts` asserts the order, that the login page is reached **even when `signOut` rejects** (without the `finally` a runner on a flaky connection presses Sign out, nothing happens, and they are still trapped), and that the teardown is awaited before the hard navigation; `signOutLink.markup.test.ts` asserts the disabled attribute, the 44px target and the two fixes above against rendered HTML. **Falsified against three source mutations**, each of which turns the suite red. A live click in a browser landed on `/auth/login`. Sign-up DOM: first name `required: false`, email and password `required: true`.. 🔴 **The `/ship` pre-ship silent-failure gate (box 1) then found a live defect this had shipped with:** `signOutAndReturnToLogin` discarded the `{ error }` that `auth.signOut()` RESOLVES with, and `GoTrueClient._signOut` returns **before** `_removeSession()` on any error that is not 401/403/404 — so a network failure left the `sb-<ref>-auth-token` cookie alive while the user was navigated to the login screen **believing they had signed out**. Exactly the founder's demo (sign out of the test account, hand the phone over). Now the error is read and `forgetLocalSession()` expires the auth cookies and localStorage keys itself; 5 tests, falsified against 3 mutations. **Residual, stated:** the access token stays valid server-side until expiry — unreachable from a device with no network, the same exposure as force-quitting the app. ⚠️ **Not in backlog — emergent, founder-raised mid-session.** ⚠️ **Nothing here has run on iOS**; the widget-store clear is a no-op on web by construction. 🔴 **AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHERE IT BROKE — the founder tested on device and sign-out threw him OUT OF THE APP INTO SAFARI**, from the wizard and from Me. Capacitor iOS (`WebViewDelegationHandler.decidePolicyFor`) allows a full-document load only if the URL `starts(with: serverURL.absoluteString)`, and `shouldAllowNavigation` consults **only** the `allowNavigation` list — **our own host is not implicitly allowed**. `server.url` carries a path (`https://www.zonna.run/dashboard`, an optimisation to skip a redirect flash), so that prefix test degenerates into *"only `/dashboard/*` counts as in-app"* and `/auth/login` was handed to `UIApplication.shared.open`. Fixed two ways: **(1)** navigation is now a client-side `router.replace` (a history navigation never reaches `decidePolicyFor`), which **ships over the air to phones already installed**; **(2)** `www.zonna.run` added to `allowNavigation`, which closes it for any future full navigation but needs a native build. ⚠️ **The rule was ALREADY WRITTEN in this repo** — `DashboardClient.tsx:255`, *"router.replace stays inside the WKWebView"* — and I overrode it an hour earlier with a confident comment justifying the hard load. Guarded by `signOutOwner.test.ts`, which now fails the build on `window.location` in the sign-out path (falsified). Blast radius surveyed: the other seven full-page navigations are either `/dashboard` (prefix-safe) or deliberately external (mailto, Stripe on web-only, Strava which already branches to `Browser.open` on native). |

Final: **7 tables covered**, 59 reads filtered. Backfilled on the one affected account: 150 completions, 66 analyses, 15 weekly reports, 17 adjustments, 12 overrides, 8 reflections — all preserved, none live. ADR-013 amended. |
| Charity access codes (GTM-CHARITY-04) | FREE (infra) — grants PAID | 2026-09-11 | Delivers "we give the app away free" to a partner's runners. A charity gets a capped batch of unique single-use codes and decides who gets one; **possession IS the verification**, because they are the only party who knows who holds a place with them, so we never identify the runners. Grants FULL PAID access, not the free tier: the free tier cannot generate a marathon plan at all (`free_tier_available: false`) and every injury-protective feature (reshaping, run analysis) is paid, which is the wrong thing to withhold from the highest injury-risk cohort the product serves (Hutchinson, SLT). Window is **race date + 7 days**: 90 days at redemption, deliberately short so the race anchor is what binds, then extend-only with an 18-month ceiling so a deferred race extends but a new race each season cannot make it perpetual. Redeem screen is full-screen, reachable from Me and Upgrade. Migration applied to production 2026-09-11. Tooling: `scripts/mint-charity-codes.ts`, `scripts/check-charity-code.ts`, runbook at `docs/runbooks/charity-codes.md`. ⚠️ **Both caveats in this cell were STALE and are corrected 2026-09-18.** The third redeem door (the wizard's distance step) shipped the same day as the GTM-CHARITY-04 follow-up in the row below, and **end-to-end redemption WAS verified against production on 2026-09-11 14:21** — 3 minted, 1 redeemed, grant running to 2026-12-10. Found by the Make-A-Wish readiness audit (`docs/partners/make-a-wish-readiness-2026-09.md`), which also corrected the roadmap's GTM-CHARITY-04 row where it described a `subscriptions`-row implementation that was never built. **Neither record is hook-watched**, which is why both rotted while every `/ship`-checked record stayed correct. |
