The device shot sat two sections down, so a first-time visitor read a headline, a paragraph, a price and a hardware caveat before seeing evidence the software exists. Now a two-column hero at the site width, copy left and product right, collapsing to one column below ~420px. Verified at 1280x800 that the phone is fully inside the first screen. Two consequential changes made deliberately: **left-aligned** (the design system asked for it all along; the centring was never a reviewed decision, GTM-SITE-01 only ruled on the tagline kicker) and **hero type reduced** to `clamp(34,3.6vw,52)` because `design_handoff_v2` raised it to 68px for a FULL-WIDTH hero and half that width reads cramped. Also: a moss **accent second line** on section titles (the competitor's rhythm device, minus the banned gradient), and the homepage's mock **coach note recoloured moss → `--warn`**, because CLAUDE.md reserves amber for coaching and the mock was throwing away the one thing the colour carried. |
| Marketing site no longer scrolls sideways on a phone (GTM-SITE-02) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | **Measured bug across every marketing page.** At a true 375px viewport the document was 433px wide: hero headlines clipped mid-word and content ran off the right edge, and mobile is exactly how a charity runner arrives from an email. Two causes. (1) The header row (wordmark + three nav items + a 104px CTA pill) does not fit one row on a phone and was already within a few pixels of overflowing with two items; below 430px the links now wrap to their own row and that row wraps again so the CTA drops to a third line on a 320px SE. Nothing hidden, no hamburger. (2) `PhoneFrame` is a fixed 340px with `flexShrink: 0` and forced the document wider; `.phone-fit` now gives the wrapper the post-scale box. **`transform: scale()` alone does NOT fix this** — it changes painting, not layout, and the first attempt overflowed by exactly as much as before. Verified across **33 page × width combinations** (320 / 375 / 414 on every marketing page): zero overflow. |
| Homepage shows the product, not a drawing of it (GTM-SITE-02 item 3) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | The homepage rendered three hand-written CSS imitations of app surfaces (`MockSessionCard`, `MockReflectCard`, `MockCoachNoteCard`) beside text cards describing the product. It now mounts the REAL `SessionCard`, `CoachNoteBlock` and `ZoneRings`, each paired under the claim it demonstrates; sample data in `lib/marketing/demoSurfaces.ts`, inset frame in `components/marketing/ProductStill.tsx`. **The backlog's stated obstacle was false and cost the item weeks of parking:** it recorded that these are `'use client'` with event handlers and would need shared presentational wrappers. None of the three carries a `'use client'` directive and none requires a handler, so a server page mounts them directly. **Rendering the real card immediately exposed a live drift** — `PhoneFrame` (hand-built by necessity) showed `8 km` in two places while `formatDistance()`, ADR-015's sole owner of distance strings, emits `8km`. Also corrected: `AnswersCard` displayed `WEEKLY_KM_CHIPS` / `LONGEST_RUN_CHIPS` band labels that the Coaching Board retired for a Ruler on 2026-08-30, a caption placed `ZoneRings` on Today when it lives on Coach, and a pre-existing 320px sideways scroll (bare `minmax(280px, 1fr)` plus the subgrid column's max-content sizing against `SessionCard`'s nowrap detail line). Guarded by `lib/marketing/realComponents.test.ts` (5 checks, each falsification-tested to RED; the PhoneFrame check passed its first falsification because the file states the distance twice, so it was rewritten to assert the absence of the wrong shape). |
| Tier resolution has one owner; comped runners stop being told their trial ended (GTM-CHARITY-04 follow-up) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | `lib/trial.ts → resolveTier(inputs, now)` is the single pure owner of `admin → subscription → charity grant → trial → free`, returning `{ tier, reason }`. The order previously existed **three** times (server, an OR-chain in `DashboardClient`, and a private `resolve()` inside `tierResolution.test.ts`), so the test asserted its own copy and could not catch a producer drifting. **The filed RevenueCat risk was stale and is closed:** grants live in `charity_codes`, not `subscriptions` (whose `provider` CHECK rejects them), verified against production indexes. **The real defect was outbound email:** `app/api/email/send-trial` had no tier check and mailed "3 days left." / "Trial ends today" to comped charity runners and to people who had already subscribed; `decideTrialEmails` now takes a REQUIRED access argument (tier + ever-held-a-grant), so the compiler forces each call site to answer. In-app, `UpgradeScreen` gained a third variant via `lib/subscriptions/upgradeFraming.ts`, and the trial countdown stopped telling a 90-day grant holder they had "11 days left in your trial". Third redeem door added to the wizard's distance step (a line, not a step), shown on day one as well as at the paywall; all three doors now return to where they were opened from. Falsification found a genuine gap in the existing suite: swapping grant below trial did NOT fail, because no case paired a LIVE grant with a LIVE trial, which is the day-one charity runner. |
| A peak rehearses the race more than one way (§104, CAT-10K-RACE-SPECIFIC-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT. 10K owned ONE race-specific row: measured, 100% of 96 10K time-target plans placed `tenk_pace_intervals` twice and no plan saw two different race-specific sessions, while `goal_pace_sharpener` (taper-only) landed zero times. Added `tenk_race_simulation` — 3 × 2km at goal pace / 90s jog, peak only, intermediate min. Plans seeing two distinct race-specific sessions **0% → 25%** (better, not solved). **Row B killed:** a float recovery at easy-moderate is Zonna Z3, the grey zone §1 exists to prevent. `INV-PLAN-RACE-SPECIFIC-VARIETY` (warn, 5.7%) asks whether repetition was a CHOICE rather than encoding a content target. **The row NAME is load-bearing** — `Session.stimulus` is stamped from the generator label, so a race_specific row named outside the "…-pace…" family is invisible to §22's own check (cost a red suite to learn). MARATHON has the identical gap, registered not fixed. |
| Distance paywall enforced at the API (TIER-ENFORCE-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | Marathon/50K/100K are PAID, and the boundary lived ONLY in `GeneratePlanScreen` — a commercial gate in a client component while ADR-003 names the API route as the auth boundary and ten other routes honour it. `canGenerateDistance` in `lib/plan/canUseFeature.ts` is now the single owner, shared by wizard and route, so the lock a runner sees and the 403 the server returns cannot disagree. `distancePaywall.test.ts` pins the ENTITLED cases hardest — trial, paid, admin, and a comped charity runner composed end-to-end through `resolveTier` — because the real risk is refusing someone who has paid, not a bypass of a plan we publish free on the website. Caught a fail-open in the first draft (`tier !== 'free'` grants any unknown tier); now an allowlist. |
| §55 rejects unrecognised enum inputs | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | §55 validated numeric ranges only. `fitness_level: 'advanced'` (not in the union) indexed a config table with no such key and killed the engine in `buildFallbackPace` — a 500 naming no field, verbatim the failure the volume-field validation was added to stop. Worse, most enum fields failed SILENTLY: a bad `recent_quality_training` matched nothing, fell to a default, and produced a real plan from an input the engine discarded. `InputEnumError` now rejects present-but-unrecognised values on all 12 enum inputs; absent stays valid (§79 runs its own assessment without `fitness_level`). Found because two measurement scripts had been passing invalid values for hours — which forced a correction to §100's published numbers. |
| Constitution section numbers are unique (principlesIntegrity) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | §55, §81 and §82 each appeared TWICE in `CoachingPrinciples.md`. Found the hard way: a Coaching Board conflict scan looked up "§81", landed on an unrelated section, and the brief put to the board asserted §81 said something it does not. That is the exact failure the conflict scan exists to prevent — with 100+ sections it resolves principles BY NUMBER. Occurrences nothing cites renumbered to §101–§103; the ones code and cross-references cite kept their numbers. Guarded by `principlesIntegrity.test.ts`. |
| A safety trim no longer hands its deficit to the next week (§100, RAMP-PRODUCER-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT. `V1-volume-quality-split` holds a week flat when the first quality/VO2max session lands; the NEXT week was built from the volume CURVE, so the trim moved the spike seven days rather than removing it (founder's live 10K: 33/33/40, +23% against a chronic load of 33). `reanchorWeekAfterTrim` now ramps from DELIVERED volume, cascading until the curve catches up. **No new numeric** — reuses §2's `MAX_WEEKLY_VOLUME_INCREASE_PCT`. Measured: 900-plan 5K/10K/HM grid **34.0% → 16.4%** breaching; full 16,038 sweep 4.0% → 3.6%. **The worst delivered rise does NOT improve (79% → 79%)** — the gain is in how often a plan breaches, not the ceiling, because the worst cases are long-run placement the engine may not trim. (First published as 22.7% → 11.5% / worst 55% → 39% from a grid carrying invalid enum values; corrected same day.) Cost in the board's own rejection metrics: mean peak 27.04 → 26.78 km, constrained 38.4% → 38.4%, maintenance 32.8% → 33.3% — the inverse of the bounceback cap rejected on 2026-09-06. §52 amendment: no easy run may go below the smallest easy run of the week just completed, so the cap partial-applies instead of emptying a week to the 4 km floor. |
| RLS defence-in-depth tooling + 5 routes converted (SEC-08) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | `lib/supabase/rlsPolicyManifest.ts` (live policy inventory; four tables have RLS on with ZERO policies, where a JWT client returns empty rather than erroring), `scripts/rls-convertible-routes.ts` (classifies all 47 API routes), `lib/supabase/rlsCoverage.test.ts` (build-time gate, three failure paths falsification-tested). Converted wizard-benchmark-estimate, strava/refresh, both OG card routes, race-times. The tooling corrected itself: a first pass cleared 22 routes and was wrong about 7, because `savePlanForUser` UPDATEs `charity_codes` via the grant re-anchor — cross-module access invisible to a regex over the route file. `pre-session-readiness` deliberately NOT converted (module-scope memoised client would leak identity across requests). |
| `daily_coach_notes` restored; coach-note cache had never worked | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | The table was committed as a migration on 2026-04-28 AND recorded in the applied-migrations ledger, and never landed in production. Both call sites discarded their error, so the cache read failed on every request and the route regenerated: every app open by a paid/trial runner paid for a fresh Anthropic call instead of one per user per day, and the call-site comment claiming otherwise had been false for months. Table applied; both sites now record `coach_note_cache_unavailable`. `npm run check:db` verifies every migration-created table actually exists (the ledger records an intention, not an outcome) and diffs the RLS manifest. Also found `core.hooksPath` unset, so a committed pre-commit security check (API auth boundary, findings 1 & 11) had been inactive locally. |
| Card elevation on standalone roots; `--section-gap` deleted (V2-POLISH-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | 16 of 53 `var(--card)` sites in DashboardClient got `--shadow-card`; the rest are nested cards, bottom sheets, sticky bars, a skeleton and an 8px dot, where elevation would be the stacked-shadow regression the design system bans. Verified afterwards that no shadowed card has a shadowed ancestor. `--section-gap: 36px` was deleted rather than adopted: read by nothing, and its value matched neither the real 20px (23 sites) nor 28px (5) rhythm, so adopting it would have been a 16px regression applied 23 times. |

---

## Tier Definitions

| Tier | Includes |
|---|---|
| FREE | Generic pre-built plan templates (5K/10K/HM, 8 & 12-week variants) via rule-based engine (no AI calls), core session display and tracking, formula-derived pace/HR targets, basic profile. No Strava, no dynamic coaching. |
| PAID | AI plan generation, dynamic plan reshaping, Strava integration, AI coaching, plan confidence score, all personalised or intelligent features. |
| Kit quotes the number on the card, not the one behind it (BUG-KIT-DECIMALS-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Founder, from a device: *"Kit says 5.7km, the plan says 6km, on the same screen."* **Measured across the 648-input cohort grid (621 plans, 25,959 sessions carrying a distance): 50.4% of prescribed session distances formatted differently for the prompt than for the card** — every half-kilometre session, which is half of them. Root cause was a documented exemption: `formatDistanceForPrompt()` returns the raw number on the km path, and its own comment said *"do not use this for anything the user reads directly."* **A number handed to the model becomes user-facing the moment the model repeats it**, so ADR-015's singularity had a hole exactly the width of the AI layer. New owner `lib/coaching/prompts/promptFormat.ts → promptDistanceFormatters(units)` splits by what the number IS: `fmtPlanned` (prescribed — delegates to `formatDistance`, so there is no second rounding rule), `fmtRace` (the race card's iconic decimal, never `42.195km`), `fmtDist` (measured/analytical, precision kept). **Ten builders now share it**; nine had declared an identical `const fmtDist = …` closure, so the classification could be right in one file and wrong in the next, and `formatDistanceForPrompt` now has exactly one caller in the prompts layer. **The raw precision was RIGHT in one place and stays:** on a planned-vs-actual comparison (`sessionFeedback` / `sessionReframe`'s "Planned distance / Actual distance", `weeklyReport`'s volume line) both sides carry 1dp — rounding the planned side up to the card's whole km would invent a shortfall the runner did not have, and the model's own few-shot is *"Cut it 2km short."* It is also what the app renders there (`{ exact: true }` on both numbers). `format.units.test.ts` had asserted the old behaviour with that §66 reasoning attached; the reasoning was right and was preserved rather than overwritten. Guard: `promptDistanceParity.test.ts` — formatters must equal `formatDistance` for the surface they mirror, real builders asserted end-to-end, and a source scan where `fmtDist` may only touch an explicit measured allowlist, so a new prompt fails until someone classifies its number. All three layers falsification-tested to RED. |
| The app shell can no longer render the marketing site (UX-AUTH-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Reported from a device as "signing out lands the app on the marketing website". The login screen and `UpgradeScreen` linked straight at `/privacy` and `/terms`, which carry `SiteHeader`/`SiteFooter`. On the web `target="_blank"` opens a tab; **inside the Capacitor webview there is no tab** — Capacitor's UI delegate loads a `_blank` navigation into the SAME webview — so the link replaced the app with the full website (Plans / Pricing / Comparisons / "Get the app") with no way back. `components/shared/ExternalLink.tsx` is now the only way out of the shell: native opens the URL in SFSafariViewController via `@capacitor/browser` (the pattern already used for OAuth), which presents over the app with its own Done button and cannot navigate the app anywhere. **`Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` is checked synchronously on purpose** — `preventDefault()` after an `await` is too late, so the first cut loaded the marketing page AND opened the sheet. The absolute URL comes from `window.location.origin`, never a constant, so it inherits the canonical host (`allowNavigation` lists `www` only; an apex URL would bounce the runner out to Safari). The other two filed candidate causes were checked in code and are not live: a signed-out app-open at `/dashboard` hits `router.replace('/auth/login')`, and no hardcoded apex URL exists anywhere in `app/`, `components/` or `lib/`. Guard: `externalLink.test.ts` **derives** the marketing route list by reading which `page.tsx` files render `SiteHeader`, so a new marketing page joins it the day it ships. |
| Password reset stops telling runners something untrue (UX-AUTH-03, code half) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | All three ways a reset link can fail rendered *"This reset link is invalid or has already been used. Request a fresh one and try again."* That is true of one of them. For the other two it is false AND a loop: when the Supabase recovery template is not configured for `token_hash`, the link carries a PKCE `?code=` whose verifier lives in the browser that asked, so every fresh link fails identically. **On iOS it can never succeed** — the request is made in the Capacitor webview and the email opens in Safari, two different browsers on the same device. `lib/auth/resetDeadEnd.ts` now names which of the three happened (`expired` / `wrong_browser` / `no_credential`) and the screen offers a new link **in place** rather than returning the runner to sign-in where the loop restarts. `lib/auth/sendPasswordReset.ts` is the single owner of `redirectTo` (derived from `window.location.origin`) and of the neutral "if that email has an account" message; login and the reset screen both use it and neither may call `resetPasswordForEmail` directly. ⚠️ **The durable fix is a dashboard setting only the account owner can make** — `docs/runbooks/password-reset.md`. The app cannot verify it: the template body is not exposed to the service-role key or to the Management API tools held here. Guard: `resetDeadEnd.test.ts` — three distinct messages, only `expired` may assert the link is invalid, every case ends in an action, no hardcoded host. |
| Sign-in asks for one decision, not four (UX-AUTH-02) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | The login screen showed Apple, Google, an OR rule, a Sign in / Sign up segmented control, two fields, a submit and "Forgot password?" to everyone, always. It now shows Apple, Google and **"Use email instead"**; the email path is disclosed on tap. Both providers were already built — this is presentation. Apple and Google stay visible once the block opens, so nothing is taken away and **Sign in with Apple keeps the prominence its HIG requires** (what is hidden is the first-party email path, not a third-party one). Fixed on the same screen: a first-time runner typing their real email and a new password on the Sign in tab got GoTrue's `"Invalid login credentials"` verbatim, which never says the account does not exist yet — the exact dead end a Make-A-Wish code-holder walks into. `lib/auth/authErrorCopy.ts` maps the two that matter (wrong-tab signin, already-registered signup) and **passes anything unrecognised through unchanged**, because a message nobody has thought about is better than a generic one that hides it. Guard: `authErrorCopy.test.ts`, including that the two remedies never cross over and that the screen no longer hands `error.message` to `setError`. No render test — `vitest.config.ts` is node-only by design, so collapsed / disclosed / sign-up were verified in a browser at 375px. **Two device-reported follow-ups, fixed same day.** (1) *"Continue with Google has lost its outline and blends into background"* — the button's fill is `--card` and it sits on a `--card` card, so the border was the only separation and `0.5px var(--line)` (8% alpha) could not carry it; now `1px var(--line-strong)`, which is what `RedeemCodeScreen`'s white-on-white input already used and which is also Google's own light-theme spec. Same defect fixed on the reset screen's secondary button, and **the rule is now written into `ui-patterns.md` beside the token**: if the fill does not separate an element from its parent, the border has to. (2) *"I can't collapse them again"* — the disclosure is now a **single button in a single place whose label flips** between "Use email instead" and "Hide email sign in", so `aria-expanded` stays attached to one element across both states; it stands where the old "OR" rule stood and does that rule's job, so the divider is gone rather than stacked above it. |
| A beginner's honest answers are accepted, and no refusal shows a field name (UX-BEGINNER-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | The wizard's longest-run slider allows **0** (`LONGEST_RUN_KM_MIN: 0`) and the API rejected `<= 0`, returning the raw exception verbatim: *"Invalid input: longest_recent_run_km=0 (acceptable range: 1-300). Empty or out-of-range physiological inputs are rejected."* **A beginner who has never run — exactly the Make-A-Wish cohort — was refused in schema language, by a database field name, for answering honestly.** Two owners disagreed about whether 0 was legal; the slider was right and the validator was wrong, so the floor moved to 0 for both `longest_recent_run_km` and `current_weekly_km` (a plan generates from 0 today — the identical plan is produced at `current_weekly_km: 1`). Every refusal now speaks in the runner's vocabulary via a `FIELD_COPY` map in `lib/plan/inputs.ts` ("We could not read how far you run in a week. Put in roughly what you cover now, and zero is fine if you are starting from scratch."), and `InputEnumError` covering the 12 enum fields lost its field-name leak in the same pass. Guard: `beginnerInputs.test.ts` asserts the field-name SHAPE never appears (`snake_case`, `field=value`) rather than banning a bare word — "your age" is exactly what a person should be told. **Filed, not fixed:** whether ~13km across 3 sessions is the right week 1 for someone who has never run is a beginner-floor question for the Coaching Board, not new and not caused by this change. |
| A first-time marathoner is no longer told their plan will not build them (MAINT-LABEL-01, copy half) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Measured across 360 beginner plans per distance: **89% of beginner MARATHON plans (320/360) carry `volume_profile: 'maintenance'`** — against 4% at half marathon and 0% at 10K — including a parkrun-er with a 24-week runway. The note said the plan *"maintains current fitness rather than building it"*, to the exact cohort going from 5km a week to 26.2 miles, on the strength of a volume ratio that says nothing about whether they will finish. All five `volume_constraint_note` variants were rewritten to be true about the same facts without the discouragement: *"This plan is built to get you to the finish, not to chase a time. You will get fitter doing it: starting from where you are, you could hardly not."* Guard: `maintenanceLabel.test.ts`. ⚠️ **The `volume_profile` VALUE is unchanged and still reads `maintenance`** — it is load-bearing (plan confidence score, §38 remedy notes) and changing it is a Coaching Board decision with a measured cohort-shape impact, not a copy edit. Copy shipped; the value is filed for a board sitting. |
| The code box stops asking for work the parser never wanted (UX-REDEEM-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | The last screen between a Make-A-Wish runner and their free access, and it implied a strict format `normaliseCode` has always been forgiving about (it accepts a code lowercase, spaced, hyphenated, with or without the prefix). **`ZONNA-` is now a static adornment** — it is known and never varies — and the separator is inserted live by `formatCodeInput`, which **shares `normaliseCode`'s own rules** so the box and the server can never disagree about what a code is. Paste from the email, type it bare, type it lowercase, type the hyphens in the wrong places: all land identically. Help text says so. **The tab bar is gone from this screen** — it was reported as still mounted underneath, so a runner mid-redemption could tap away to Today/Plan/Coach and lose the flow; `ui-patterns.md` allows a full screen (replaces the tab bar) or a sheet (mirrored nav), and this was neither. Guard: `codeInput.test.ts`, including a round-trip over 50 minted codes asserting the box and `normaliseCode` never diverge. |
| `cohort:shape` — the regression that catches a population being reclassified (COHORT-SHAPE-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | **The third verification question, and the one that had no answer.** `npm run verify` proves plans are VALID; `verify:parity` proves they are UNCHANGED; **neither can see a cohort being reclassified.** Extending §81's obligation to structured sessions took `volume_profile: 'maintenance'` from 20% to 80% of plans at a 30-minute weekday cap — a **+60pp** swing, the same magnitude the Coaching Board had REJECTED five days earlier — and the suite stayed green throughout: 16,038 plans, zero violations, nothing to see. Runs an **exhaustive, un-sampled, clock-independent** 648-input grid (`lib/plan/cohortGrid.ts`) and diffs rates against a committed baseline: maintenance, constrained-by-inputs, volume-constrained, time-compressed, constraint-note, no-quality, early-onset, mean quality per build week, mean delivered peak, mean plan length, **and maintenance per distance** (an aggregate holds flat while marathon swings). Enforced on every build by `cohortShape.test.ts` inside `npm run verify`. ⚠️ **A defect found in its own first cut:** it re-summed `distance_km` to get delivered peak, but a beginner's plan is **duration-anchored** (`duration_mins` set, `distance_km` null), so it understated the peak by 30% (30.13 against the true 39.26). It now reads the engine's own `Week.weekly_km`. Re-baseline with `-- --write` and say in the commit which number moved and why; **never to turn a test green.** Falsification-tested against the real §81 incident, a volume-cap change, and a grid that stops generating. |
| A marathon peak meets goal pace somewhere other than the long run (§105, CAT-MARATHON-RACE-SPECIFIC-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Measured across 96 generated marathon time-target plans: **100% reused `mp_long_run`, up to three times in a single plan** (216 placements / 96 plans). `mp_long_run` is a `long_run_with_segment`, so **every scrap of marathon-specific work was embedded in the long run** — a runner never met goal pace except on long-run day, and one whose long run is already the week's constraint got marathon-pace exposure nowhere else. Identical thinness to the 10K case ruled the same day (§104). Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT: `mp_blocks` ("Marathon-pace blocks", reps × 4km at goal pace, 3 min jog) added as a standalone peak-eligible row. ⚠️ **The session NAME is load-bearing** — `Session.stimulus` is stamped from the label, which is how an earlier "Broken 10K" candidate failed every 10K plan it touched. A proposed load guard alongside this was **WITHDRAWN, not forced**: it failed 228 then 57 tests on board-sanctioned behaviour (§22 renames, and HM has paired `hm_pace_long_run` with `hm_pace_intervals` since R23), and in failing it exposed that §104's own invariant was mis-scoped — it counted the long run and fired on 92% of marathon plans. |
| A structured session overrunning the weekday cap says so, and does not silently downgrade the plan (§81 amendment, MWM-STRUCTURED-MAINTENANCE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | §81 exempts the long run **and structured sessions** from `max_weekday_mins`, and the exemption carries an obligation: past 50% overrun the plan must SAY SO and CLASSIFY maintenance. The engine applied it to the long run only (`if (!isLongRun(sn)) continue`). The defect fix shipped **both** halves, and measuring afterwards showed maintenance going **20% → 80% at a 30-minute cap (+60pp)**, with 60% of those plans carrying the new note. **The downgrade was reverted and the note kept.** §81's maintenance clause was written for the long-run case, which §81 itself calls rare (896 plans, median overrun 127%); the structured case is 60% of cap-30 plans, and the same remedy at sixty times the incidence is a different decision from the one §81 recorded — the exact magnitude this board rejected on 2026-09-06. The substance is arguable too: **weekends are not capped**, so a runner with 30-minute weekdays and a free Sunday can build perfectly well for a 10K, and calling that "maintenance" over-classifies the cohort §81's own closing paragraph (Sims, caregiving loads) exists not to turn away. Silence is fixed; classification stays at its baseline. **This is the incident that produced `cohort:shape`.** |
| The Plan row is quiet, and says a session's name once (UX-PLAN-MOVE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Founder: *"we have 'move' text on sessions... I don't like that."* The `↕ Move` pill — a moss-soft accent pill with a word, on every movable row — is now a bare `↕` in `--mute` beside the row's own `›` chevron, sharing that vocabulary as a handle rather than a CTA; moss is reserved for CTA and active states (ADR-007) and spending it per-row devalued it. Hit area is 44×44 (iOS HIG) with negative margins so row height is unchanged; the old pill was ~24px. **The backlog's constraint on this item was wrong and is corrected in the code comment:** it said the earlier 3-line handle "caused a documented 2026-06-26 incident" and that reverting "re-opens a real defect". RESHAPE-FIX-WAVE2C shipped three things and the safety is in the other two — `pendingMove` stages the move and an inline confirmation row must be confirmed before any write. An accidental tap costs a Cancel and zero DB side-effect. The hamburger is still not coming back: `≡` is the universal "menu" glyph, the exact ambiguity Defect 11 named. **Found while verifying and fixed in the same pass:** the row also carried a type chip reading `SESSION_LABELS[session.type]` which took **115px of a 172px row and clipped the label to 51px** — every easy day read "Easy…". Measured over **5,280 generated sessions**: 65.7% of chips were a verbatim duplicate of the label; on a long run it was WRONG (`type: 'easy'`, so "Long run — marathon pace + HM-pace finish" wore "Easy run — Zone 2"); on quality it was less informative than the label. Removed; re-measured at **0 rows clipped**. D9 had already asserted "the session label is primary, let the chip yield first" and set `flexShrink: 1` — it never fired, because the label is `flex: 1 1 0%` so there is never negative free space to shrink. Guard: `planRowLabel.test.ts`, a source assertion plus the measurement kept executable so the reason cannot rot. |
| One owner for "how far is this session" (SESSION-KM-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | A session is anchored EITHER by distance OR by duration, and beginners get duration-anchored plans (`duration_mins` set, `distance_km` null). The engine always knew this — `sumWeeklyKm` reads `distance_km ?? duration_mins / minPerKmEasy` — but that expression was hand-written in **six** places, and a second, incompatible answer to the same question, `distance_km ?? 0`, in **fourteen** more across `ruleEngine`, `invariants`, `cohortShape` and `reshapeMagnitude`. `?? 0` is not a safe default: it asserts "this session covered no ground", which on a duration-anchored plan is wrong for every session. Three measured defects came from that one expression — §24's peak long-run floor reading **0 km for a 132-minute long run**; the `cohort:shape` harness understating mean delivered peak by 30% on its first cut; and a measurement concluding a `current_weekly_km: 0` runner gets an empty week 1. `lib/plan/sessionDistance.ts` is now the single owner; `easyMinPerKm` is passed in because the engine holds a `PaceGuide` and the validator derives from `meta.vdot`, and a caller with no pace gets **`null`, not 0**, so "unknown" cannot be read as "zero" a fourth time. The six correct copies moved onto it in a behaviour-neutral commit: `verify:parity` IDENTICAL, 2,916 cases byte-for-byte. The fourteen `?? 0` sites are filed as SESSION-KM-02 — two of them silently disable §47 long-run alternation and §52 easy-floor protection for beginners, so they need a board ruling, not a sweep. |

Fixed via SESSION-KM-01; it now reads "Peak long run 16.5 km", and `cohort:shape` confirms the classification was **correct all along** (49.8%, per-distance identical) — the reason was false, not the verdict. | A plan never peaks below where the runner already is (§106, Coaching Board MAINT-PROFILE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | `peakKmByLevel` read race distance and fitness level and **never asked what the runner already runs**. This adds none: `startKm` is already the declared volume and §106 only refuses a curve whose top is below its own start; §2's 10% rule, §45's long-run cap and §3's deload cadence stay fully binding, and §10/CD-6's `<6mo` over-claim cap still governs `startKm` first. **§79 wins where they meet** — an upward level declaration is excluded, keeping `INV-PLAN-USER-LEVEL-NO-UPWARD-TONNAGE`'s veto intact; the board's conflict scan missed §79 and the engine's invariant caught it. **`PEAK_KM_BY_LEVEL` moved from `lib/plan/length.ts` into `GENERATION_CONFIG`** in the same commit: 18 coaching numerics setting the most consequential figure in a plan had no principle, were invisible to `configPrincipleSync`, and did not trip the coaching-guard hook. Values unchanged. Guard: `INV-PLAN-PEAK-NOT-BELOW-START` (warn), measuring the **DELIVERED** week not the target (ADR-022's finding restated) and **deliberately NOT excusable by `maintenance`**, unlike §23/§46/§52 — a detraining block is not an honest response to a constraint. **Measured: 92 of 621 cohort plans get a higher peak, ZERO get a lower one** (largest rise +18 km, verified empirically against a stashed baseline, not asserted from the construction); parity 814/2916 changed, deliberate; mean delivered peak 39.26 → 39.96 km; maintenance 49.8% → 51.0%; sweep 16,038 plans / 0 violations. The residual warn fires on 28.1% and is filed, not hidden. |
| Beginners stop running with less enforcement than everyone else (SESSION-KM-02) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | Measured before touching anything: **95.8% of a beginner's sessions are duration-anchored (`duration_mins` set, `distance_km` null), against 0% for intermediate and experienced** — so `distance_km ?? 0` is a beginner-only defect. **Quality sessions are never duration-anchored (0 of 3,678)**, so four sites in `invariants.ts` comparing quality kilometres were measured unreachable and deliberately left alone; `race_distance_km ?? 0` is a plan field, excluded for the same reason. **The failure mode was SILENT PASS, not false fire.** Four checks never ran for beginners: the injury-capped delivered long-run cap, its healthy weekly-increase twin, peak long-run alternation (every peak read 0, so alternation passed vacuously), and `INV-PLAN-FOUNDATION-BLOCK`'s long-run share — foundation blocks being almost entirely a beginner surface (10,881 in the sweep). All four now run and find **zero violations**: the engine was already compliant, the checks were not looking. **The serious one was `reshapeMagnitude.ts`** — `const beforeKm = distance_km ?? 0` then `if (beforeKm > 0)` made ADR-012's >15% structural-change threshold unreachable for a duration-anchored session, so **a beginner's session could be trimmed silently where an experienced runner's identical trim surfaced a confirmation tile**: the 2026-06-26 incident class reintroduced for the cohort least able to notice. Now compared on whichever axis the session is anchored. `sessionKmSelfPaced()` joined `lib/plan/sessionDistance.ts` rather than a third copy of parse-and-divide appearing across three files; it converts via the session's own pace band and returns `null`, never 0, when it cannot. Guard: `sessionDistanceReach.test.ts` keeps the reachability measurement executable so the exclusions cannot rot. `verify:parity` IDENTICAL, 2,916 cases — no prescription changed. **Two prescription sites remain board-gated and their impact is explicitly NOT yet measured** (§47 alternation and §52's easy floor, both inert for beginners). |

Recorded as closed so it is not re-proposed. **(2) `brand.md`'s audience line went from *"adult runners, 1+ years' experience"* to *"adult runners with a day job"*.** The qualifier was **never in the internal positioning sentence**, which is purely behavioural (*"runners who always go hard on their easy days… no business training like professionals"*) — and a first-time charity marathoner meets that description MORE completely than an experienced runner, not less: they hero their sessions early with a fundraising page watching, which is the exact mechanism the product exists to interrupt. The qualifier sat only in the demographic sketch, which went stale the moment a referral channel started sending first-timers. ⚠️ **It would have been an overclaim the same morning.** The roadmap's "beginner gap" named two live defects — the wizard refusing an honest zero, and 89% of beginner marathon plans reading *you will not improve* — both fixed 2026-09-11, alongside three more found the same day (§24 reporting 0 km peak long runs, four invariants silently inert on duration-anchored plans, and ADR-012's structural-change threshold unreachable for beginners). **The audience widened because the product earned it that day.** **Still NOT claimed:** Zonna is not couch-to-5K — it does not teach someone to run, and its value starts at "you are training for a race and going too hard at it". Two beginner questions stay open with the Coaching Board (the week-1 floor, and §47/§52 inert on duration-anchored plans). Surfaces corrected where they asserted the old line in the present tense: `brand.md`, the `charity-runners/page.tsx` header comment (it described the page as serving a cohort the brand excluded), the roadmap's beginner-gap section, and both GTM-CHARITY-01 shipped-records. Superseded v1 docs left alone. |
| Comping a charity runner actually comps them (GTM-CHARITY-03) | FREE (infra) — grants PAID | 2026-09-11 | RevenueCat's webhook is the single writer of `subscriptions`, which `getUserTier` reads, but `toStatus()` handled only seven lifecycle events. **A promotional entitlement granted from the RevenueCat dashboard arrives as `NON_RENEWING_PURCHASE`, hit `default`, and the route replied "received" while writing nothing** — so a runner we had comped stayed `tier: 'free'`, still met the marathon paywall, and nothing anywhere recorded that it had happened. `NON_RENEWING_PURCHASE` and `SUBSCRIPTION_EXTENDED` now map to `'active'`. **Unknown events still do nothing, deliberately** — guessing a status from an event we do not understand would mis-tier a real paying customer — but they now record `revenuecat_event_unhandled`, so the branch is visible instead of silent. | Coach stops crashing: React error 310 in TrendCard (HOOKS-ORDER-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 (deployed + **confirmed on device** 2026-09-12) | ✅ **Founder confirmed Coach loads on a real signed-in account, 2026-09-12** — the runner-facing verification, not just a green deploy. The crash only ever hit accounts whose aerobic trend resolves, which is exactly the path no harness here can reach, so this confirmation is the evidence. Committed `d1f40b1` on 2026-09-11; **reached production on 2026-09-12** (`dpl_7Natt…`, verified as the deployment aliased to `www.zonna.run`). The Vercel Hobby 100-deploys/day cap was hit minutes before the fix landed (DEPLOY-QUOTA-01), so **Coach went on crashing for 20 hours after it was fixed** — a push is not a deploy. `TrendCard` called `useCountUp` twice at the BOTTOM of the component, below the skeleton / locked / pending early returns. `useCountUp` is itself a hook containing **five** hooks, so the component rendered **1 hook in skeleton state and 11 in live state**. On Coach it mounts as `skeleton` while the aerobic trend loads and flips to `live` when it lands — **1 → 11 hooks on the next render** is exactly React error 310, and it took the whole screen down through the error boundary. ⚠️ **It only hit runners with enough history for the trend to RETURN** — a runner whose trend never resolves stays on the skeleton branch and never sees it. That is also why it could not be reproduced in a harness: without a session the trend fetch 401s, the card never leaves skeleton, and the hook count never changes. Fix: both calls hoisted above every early return, live fields read through a narrowed local. `useCountUp`'s deps moved `[]` → `[target, duration]` — **required, not tidying**: hoisted, a skeleton render passes target 0, and with `[]` the animation would run to zero once and never move again. **Found by `rules-of-hooks`, which had never run here:** `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` is already installed as a Next dependency but the repo has no eslint config, so a whole class of crash was invisible to every gate. Wiring it in is HOOKS-LINT-01; it also found the same defect latent in `TodayScreen` (HOOKS-ORDER-02). |
| The error boundary says what actually broke (ERRORBOUNDARY-MESSAGE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | The boundary had **always captured `error.message` into state and never rendered it**. The app knew exactly what had failed and showed the runner "Something went wrong" — so the most detailed report anyone could give was that sentence, and a whole debugging session went on a Coach crash the screen could have named in one line. Now renders the message in an inset panel **to everyone** (a runner who can read the error back is worth more than a tidier error screen, and there is nothing sensitive in a React error), adds **Copy details** for message + component stack, and **names chunk errors** — a bundle chunk that no longer exists is the classic symptom of a deploy landing while an app is open, and "The app updated while you had it open. Reloading will fix this" is a real instruction rather than a shrug. Also dropped `data-theme="dark"`, dead since ADR-008, and moved three legacy aliases to Warm Slate tokens. Verified by throwing a TypeError inside the boundary in a browser; no render-test infrastructure exists (vitest is node-only by design). |
| Coach stops dereferencing a week that is not there (COACH-NULLWEEK-01) | FREE | 2026-09-11 | `getCurrentWeek` ends `return past ?? weeks[0]`, which is `undefined ?? undefined` for an EMPTY array, and `DashboardClient` calls it as `getCurrentWeek(plan?.weeks ?? [])`. The Coach block then optional-chained `currentWeek` on one line — proving the author knew it could be null — and dereferenced it raw on the next two (`.sessions`, `.date`). Reproduced against the real function: `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'sessions')`. Coach is the only screen that derives a WEEK and dereferences it (`if (!plan) return null` covers Today and Plan), which is why it presented as Coach-only. Now returns `CoachTeaser` — the honest fallback for "there is no week to coach". Guard: `currentWeekGuard.test.ts`, falsification-tested. **Found while hunting a different crash and shipped on its own merits; it was not the reported bug.** |
| Invariant liveness: prove each rule can still be made to fail (INVARIANT-LIVENESS-01, test-coverage proposal #5) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | `verify:invariants` proves 93 invariants are registered, declared, and do not FALSE-fire on three canonical cases. It says nothing about the opposite and more important property: that a rule can fire **at all**. This repo has shipped a green tick with nothing behind it more than once (`--section-gap`, the decorative-config family, D9's `flexShrink` that could never fire, §97's two inert gates). ⚠️ **The obvious measurement is the wrong one, and it was built first:** 86 of 93 invariants never fire across 621 valid generated plans — and that is exactly what a HEALTHY engine looks like, because an invariant is meant to be silent. Silence cannot tell a working rule from a dead one. So `lib/plan/invariantLiveness.ts` deliberately BREAKS valid plans **42 ways** (strip a field, invert the volume curve, make every week a peak, make the long run 90% of the week) and records which rules wake up. **33 of 93 are proven wakeable.** The other 60 are **UNPROVEN, not proven dead**, and are baselined in `lib/plan/__fixtures__/invariantLivenessBaseline.json` with a reason: `corpus` (11 — the harness never builds that plan SHAPE; maintenance has its own generator, and foundation / recalibration / ultra sit outside the cohort grid), `mutation` (1 — the battery does not perturb the field that rule reads yet), `unclassified` (48 — **the column that should shrink**). Enforced by `invariantLiveness.test.ts` inside `npm run verify`: a NEW invariant that nothing can wake fails the build, so the next one proves itself on the way in. The baseline may only shrink. Same debt-register pattern as SWEEP-BASELINE-01. Standalone: `npm run invariant:liveness`. |
| A commit hook that checks a ship actually reached the registry and the build-log (SHIP-RECORD-CHECK-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-11 | PostToolUse hook on `git commit`: for a `feat(`/`fix(` commit that changed source, it checks that the item named in the commit's **scope** gained a **feature-registry row** and a **build-log entry** — the `/ship` move, which is the step that gets skipped because by the time it comes round the code is already finished. **Measured the day it was written:** the same manual doc audit ran three times in one day and found a gap every time, and a fourth pass by this hook found three more that all three manual passes had missed (GTM-CHARITY-01 / -03 / GTM-SEO-PLANS-01). A manual audit is scoped to the items you remember working on; this reads git history. ⚠️ **It checks STRUCTURE, not presence:** the ID must appear in a registry row's FIRST CELL and in a build-log `##` heading — the `/ship` template's own format. A plain grep called GTM-CHARITY-02 covered because its ID appeared inside *another* feature's row. ⚠️ **It reads the subject's scope and never the body:** the first cut scanned the whole message and fired on a commit that FILED a new open item, demanding a registry row for something deliberately left open. A hook that cries wolf gets disabled, which is the same as having no hook. Tuned against all 48 of that day's ship commits: **0 false positives.** `.claude/hooks/ship-record-check.py`; tests `python3 .claude/hooks/ship-record-check.test.py`. |
| `rules-of-hooks` runs on every build (HOOKS-LINT-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-12 | `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` has shipped with Next for the life of this repo and **there was no eslint config at all**, so the rule had never once run over this code. It found HOOKS-ORDER-01 (the live Coach crash) in about three seconds after an hour of manual searching, and HOOKS-ORDER-02 latent in `TodayScreen` in the same pass. `.eslintrc.hooks.json` enables **only** `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` — the repo has deliberately never adopted a full lint config and this does not smuggle one in. `npm run verify:hooks` runs it over `app` + `components` + `lib`, placed immediately after `typecheck` in the `verify` chain because it is the cheapest gate in it. ⚠️ **`noInlineConfig: true`, and that is load-bearing twice over.** It drops 29 errors from `eslint-disable` comments naming rules this config does not load (a gate that fails for reasons unrelated to hooks gets disabled), **and** it means the gate cannot be silenced by an inline disable in the code it polices. `--quiet` drops the resulting advisory warnings so the output speaks only about hooks. **Shipped with an EMPTY debt register** — HOOKS-ORDER-02 was fixed first so no baseline was needed, unlike SWEEP-BASELINE-01 and the liveness baseline. **Liveness-proved on every run** by `lib/hooksGate.test.ts`: a temp fixture with a hook below an early return must make the gate exit non-zero naming the rule, and the same hook above the early return must exit zero. A gate that only ever passes is indistinguishable from one that cannot fire. |
| `TodayScreen`'s empty-plan guard moved below its fourteen hooks (HOOKS-ORDER-02) | FREE | 2026-09-12 | The defect that crashed Coach, latent in the screen a Make-A-Wish runner sees first. `if (!currentWeek) return (…)` sat at `DashboardClient.tsx:6620` above **fourteen** hooks (`useState` ×5, `useMemo` ×4, `useRef` ×2, `useEffect` ×2, `useCallback` ×1), so a `currentWeek` that flipped between renders changed the hook COUNT and took the screen down with React error 310. **Latent, not live** — `weekIndex` addresses an existing week on every normal path — which is why it was filed rather than rushed in while Coach was down. Fixed by moving the RETURN below the last hook rather than hoisting fourteen hooks above it, and making the four `currentWeek` reads in between defensive. ⚠️ **`parseLocalDate((currentWeek as any).date)` was the real crash line**, not the hook count: `parseLocalDate` calls `.split` on its argument, so an absent week threw a TypeError before React ever got to the hook-order error. `as any` is why the compiler never said so. `plan.weeks[0]` carried the identical throw for an empty plan and was fixed with it. None of those values reach the DOM — the guard still returns the same "Unable to load plan" UI, just after the hooks have run. Enforced from now on by HOOKS-LINT-01. |

What was drifting was the NARRATIVE, told twice in two files: `/about` had *"a plateau that WOULD NOT move"* and *"easy days WERE NOT easy, hard days WERE NOT hard, AND everything ended up…"*, the app had *"WOULDN’T move"*, *"weren’t easy. Hard days weren’t hard."* and an em dash. Same story, two wordings, no way to tell which was canonical. ⚠️ **Worse: `"You’re trying hard. That’s the problem."` — CLAUDE.md’s stated Core Truth — was hardcoded in BOTH files with DIFFERENT apostrophe entities (`&rsquo;` vs `&apos;`), so one sentence rendered as two different characters.** It is now `BRAND.coreTruth`, which is where brand doctrine said it belonged all along. `lib/marketing/founderStory.ts` is the single owner. **Canonical wording chosen on the record, not by whichever file was opened last:** the app’s short declaratives over the web’s comma-joined clause (brand voice is "one sentence is better than two", and contractions are how this brand speaks), and the web’s full stop over the app’s em dash (founder call 2026-09-11 — the app sits outside the `noEmDash` test only because engine labels have a live coupling, which a hand-authored note does not). The new module is in `noEmDash.test.ts`’s SURFACES list, so moving the strings out of the page did not move them out of enforcement. Guarded by `founderStoryOwnership.test.ts` — both surfaces must render from the owner and neither may hardcode the narrative or the core truth; **falsification-proved by re-inlining a paragraph and watching it go red.** Rendered and read end-to-end at `/about`. |
| What a run scores, and what stays outside it (§108, UX-POSTRUN-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-12 | **Coaching Board, SPLIT ruling. Ratifies values that already ship — no runner-visible change.** `scoreSession()` has always collapsed four axes into one 0–100 `total_score` on a fixed ratio (`hr_discipline` 0.50 / `distance` 0.25 / `pace` 0.15 / `ef` 0.10), banded nailed ≥80 / close ≥60 / off_target ≥40 / concerning <40. 🔴 **The Configuration Singularity was bypassed by a file path for the SECOND time.** Those numerics live in `lib/coaching/constants.ts` and `configPrincipleSync.test.ts` read **only** `GENERATION_CONFIG` — so numbers deciding whether a runner is told "nailed" or "concerning" had **no principle and no mechanical check**. That is `peakKmByLevel` (§106) verbatim, the lesson CLAUDE.md already records. The check now reads both files and matches on `GROUP.key`, so a group name cannot document four separate coaching decisions with one word; §108 names the literal keys for that reason. Two new arms: the weights must **sum to 1.0** (a weighting that does not silently rescales every score and moves every verdict band without changing a band value), and the bands must descend inside 0–100. **It proved it can go red in the act of being written** — it failed until §108 named `hr_discipline` rather than describing it in prose. 📌 **Subjective inputs stay OUTSIDE the score.** RPE and fatigue are collected, stored and shown, and are not weighted in. Hutchinson: folding them in asserts an exchange rate between how hard a session felt and how disciplined the HR was, and no such rate is known. Sims, independently: subjective load carries signal the device does not — particularly across the menstrual cycle and in under-fuelled runners — and averaging it into a composite is how that signal stops being actionable. The filed question ("four numbers → one") is at **INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE**: those four numbers could not be found in the product. |
| A progress surface remembers and compares, it does not predict (§109, UX-COACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | **Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT**, on the founder's ask for "where I was, where I am, and what the potential is". Two of those three already shipped — R31's `baselineSeconds` / `currentSeconds` / `deltaSeconds` with four confidence states. **Only the third invents anything, and the board ruled it must not.** "Potential" is the runner's **own `target_time`**, framed by `meta.goal_beyond_measured_fitness` (§44 Amdt) as within reach or demanding on current evidence. **Rejected: any projected race-day finish, any rising line toward a race date, any percentage.** §44.1 governs directly — *"the engine cannot defend a probability; a '72% chance' is fabricated precision, and false precision is an overclaim"* — and a projected finish time is that claim wearing a different number. Hutchinson: fitness does not extrapolate linearly, and the last eight weeks of improvement are the **worst** predictor of the next eight because the early gains are the cheap ones. Confidence must sit **in the copy, not a tooltip** (Sims — a woman in the luteal phase, under-fuelled, sees an estimate reflecting the day rather than her fitness). **The regression case ships with it or it does not ship** (McMillan — a surface that only speaks when the arrow is up is marketing). Enforced by `raceProjectionHonesty.test.ts`: the race-times contract carries no future-dated field, and no runner-facing copy makes a forward-looking claim. Mechanically checkable **only because the copy is centralised** in `raceProjectionsCopy.ts` — so the test also pins that centralisation, since a component that started hardcoding strings would narrow the check to nothing without failing it. Both matchers are falsification-proved in-file. |

A comment is not a mechanism. This repo has watched the identical shape fail four times: the deload cadence in five places, tier resolution in three, `distance_km ?? 0` in twenty, and the display zone derived two ways. | Kit stops telling a runner they improved when they did not, about the wrong session type (TREND-DIRECTION-01, UX-COACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | 🔴 **A SECOND fault in the same sentence: it named the wrong session.** It read *"**Easy** is easier than it was"* while being fed `trendCardData`, which fetches `session_type: 'long'` — and a card labelled **"Easy run trend"** renders directly below it from `easyTrendData` (`session_type: 'easy'`), a **different cohort with different numbers**. Kit made a claim about easy running from long-run data, beside a card of the same name showing something else. The fold now prefers the easy-run trend (it is what the hero line is about, and it agrees with the card sharing its name) and falls back to the long-run trend, which then *says* long run. Subject, verb and pronoun travel together in one table rather than being derived at the point of use — deriving agreement is how you end up patching "they was" with a string replace. 🔴 **A live false statement, found by designing the regression case first.** The CO-ONE read folded in one hardcoded sentence — *"Easy is easier than it was — {earlierHr} **down to** {nowHr} since {month}"* — guarded only by the presence of a model-written `gloss`. **The gloss is produced whenever `hrIsTrending`, which is `Math.abs(hrDeltaBpm) >= MIN_HR_DELTA_BPM` — absolute value, so it fires in BOTH directions.** A runner whose easy heart rate had risen 4+ bpm at the same pace was therefore told *"Easy is easier than it was — 147 down to 152"*: a claim contradicted by the two numbers inside the same sentence. Same family as the "stepped volume up from 32 to 32 km" defect — the engine did the right thing and then described it wrongly. `trendSentence()` is now the owner and is direction-aware. A flat trend deliberately reads as the improvement branch, because "held steady" is honest and is the kinder of the two readings. The regression line states the fact and gives the benign reading first — *"Easy is costing you more than it was… That is common mid-build, and worth watching if it holds"* — because a rising easy HR mid-build is usually load, not decline, and `--danger` is for errors, never training UI. **Found because the Coaching Board made the regression case BINDING** (McMillan: *"a surface that can only speak when the arrow is up is marketing"*). It was not silent on regression; it was confidently wrong. 7 tests, including a falsification arm that reproduces the shipped template so the file fails loudly if anyone reinstates it, and a test pinning the `Math.abs` that is the root cause. |

| The race arc reaches a runner who never took a benchmark (RACE-ARC-REACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | 🔴 **Reported three times before I checked the database instead of the code.** The arc was built, shipped, tested and looked at — and could fully draw on **2 of 17 live plans**. The founder's could draw **none of it**: race 100 km (ultra, VDOT cannot project it), `meta.vdot` null (no benchmark, so no baseline), goal `finish` (no target time). Zero of three points. Across all live plans: **10 have no `meta.vdot`**, 4 are ultras, 7 could show "was → now", 2 could show all three. **(1) A derived baseline** — `lib/coaching/fitnessBaseline.ts` takes the earliest 6-week window of qualifying aerobic runs, requiring 3+ runs and **6 weeks of separation** from the current window so the arc is not comparing a runner with themselves a fortnight ago. §109 permits it: working out what fitness WAS, from runs actually done then, is **remembering**, and it uses the same method as the "now" point, which makes the comparison MORE like-for-like than benchmark-versus-aerobic-runs. Labelled with its **month**, never "Plan start" — run data can begin long after the plan did. `weightedAerobicSpeed` is now the single owner of the distance-weighting formula the route had written out by hand. **(2) Ultras get an arc**, at the nearest projectable standard distance, labelled. Refusing a 100 km finish time and refusing the runner's whole trajectory are two different refusals, and only the first is honest — the card **already prints** their marathon row. 🔴 **AND THE BIGGER FIND: state 1 was arithmetically guaranteed to report a regression.** It compared raw `meta.vdot` against its own DISCOUNTED self, described in the old comment as *"pre-discount for fair comparison"*. `applyVdotDiscount` always discounts — 5% floor, growing every 4 weeks of staleness — so the present was always slower than the past. **Every benchmark runner who had not re-tested was being told they had got slower, by a margin that grew the longer they left it.** Survivable as a small delta chip; not survivable as the hero arc it was about to become. One measurement is one point; state 1 now passes no baseline. `raceBaselineHonesty.test.ts`. ✅ **Verified by replaying the route's decision path against the founder's 17 real qualifying rows** — derived baseline 10 runs from Apr at 2.258 m/s, current 2 runs at 2.574 m/s, arc at marathon **4:26:56 → 3:53:36, 33m 20s faster**. The "now" value matches the marathon row in the founder's screenshot exactly, so the replay is faithful. The 6-week current window is byte-for-byte unchanged: this adds a past point, it does not move anyone's projected times. |
| The trend card stops claiming what the cohort cannot support (TREND-PACE-CLAIM-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | 🔴 **A live overclaim, shipped the same day and caught by asking what the cohort actually controls for.** `buildHrTrendSeries` matches on **DISTANCE only** (±`DISTANCE_TOLERANCE_PCT`) plus a date window. The explanation sheet said the comparison was *"at the same pace"* **twice**, with the conclusion *"your aerobic base is growing"* hanging off it. Distance and pace are independent on an easy run, so the cohort controlled the variable that does not matter and left free the one that does. ⚠️ **The failure is not random, and that is the finding.** Hutchinson at the SLT: a runner complying with Zonna **is slowing down**, so the runners most likely to be told something false are the ones the product is working for. Inverse correlation with adherence, not an edge case. `paceContext()` in `trendSentence.ts` is the single owner of "does pace undermine what the heart rate appears to say", read **signed** — a materially slower pace with a falling HR confounds, a materially faster pace with a falling HR confirms. `Math.abs` here would collapse the two into one verdict, which is TREND-DIRECTION-01 exactly. The claim is **withheld, the data is not**: the runner still gets both numbers plus the reason the obvious reading does not hold, because silence would be McMillan's *"surface that can only speak when the arrow is up"*. The sparkline's moss follows the same owner, so the line cannot celebrate a fall the sentence just refused to credit. 📊 **The threshold was MEASURED, not inherited.** `TREND_SERIES.MIN_PACE_DELTA_SEC` (5 s/km) answers a different question for the reframe prompt and sits far below the noise floor. Production, 2026-09-12, aerobic runs in a ±15% distance band: **within-month SD of easy pace 40.2 s/km**, mean month-to-month shift **21.9 s/km** — a 5 s/km gate is an eighth of the SD and would have silenced the card permanently. New `TREND_PACE_CONFOUND_SEC_PER_KM = 20`. 🔴 **The value is PROVISIONAL and labelled as such in the constant**: 3 users, 19 runs in band, 4 comparable month pairs. The SCALE argument is robust at any sample size; the number 20 is not. Re-measure at cohort scale. Copy now states what is held constant (similar distance) and names what is not (pace), guarded mechanically by `trendCopyHonesty.test.ts` — a source grep with both falsification arms, closing the gap the SLT flagged: `raceProjectionHonesty.test.ts` polices the race copy tree and nothing policed this one, which is where the wrong claim lived. All pace strings via `formatPace(secPerKm, units)` per ADR-015, unit-aware. 20 tests. |
| The line between the two numbers (TREND-SPARKLINE-01, UX-COACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | Founder, recognising it from a mockup: *"there was a graph which showed a line. I like that."* **The data was already in the payload.** `/api/coaching/trend` returns the full monthly bucket series; the Coach fetch read `buckets[0]` and `buckets[last]` and dropped everything between, so the card could say "152 → 144" and could not say whether that was a steady drift or a spike and a recovery, which is the only interesting question about a trend. `lib/coaching/trendSparkline.ts` owns the geometry, and carries **two honesty rules that are easy to break silently**: (1) **x is elapsed TIME, not array position** — buckets are sparse by construction (`buildHrTrendSeries` drops any month under `MIN_RUNS_PER_BUCKET`), so a runner who logged in April and not again until September must see one long flat span, not a step identical to April→May; (2) **a month with no usable value is dropped, never interpolated** — since the HR plausibility gate landed, `avgHr` is null whenever a month held nothing a human produced while running. **Refuses to draw below three points** (`SPARKLINE_MIN_POINTS`): two points are a straight segment between numbers the card already shows in 44pt type, and drawing it implies a continuity the data has not demonstrated (§44.1). A flat series sits on the CENTRE line rather than pinned to an edge, so a series that did nothing cannot render as an all-time high. Falling is moss, rising is muted — a rising easy HR mid-build is usually load, not decline, and `--danger` is errors only, never training UI. 18 tests, falsification-proved against array-position spacing and edge-pinned flats. |
| The race progress arc, built rather than commented (UX-COACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | 🔴 **Caught by the founder on a real device: "I don't see the pace progression."** The previous ship moved `RaceTimesCard` to Coach, wrote a comment block titled *"THE ARC — where I was · where I am · the goal I chose"* above it, changed **nothing inside the component**, and I reported it delivered. The card went on rendering one number and a compressed delta chip. **Two of the three points already existed and were being thrown away:** `baselineSeconds` was computed by the route, sent in the payload and typed by the card, and **drawn by nothing** — the runner's starting point reached the client and never reached the screen. The third did not exist at all: the route had never read `meta.target_time`. Now: `lib/coaching/raceProgressArc.ts` owns the SHAPE (which points exist, signed direction, the goal gap clamped at zero) as a pure function with 20 tests, falsified against the two families it guards — `Math.abs` direction-blindness and an unclamped gap rendering "-3m to go". `components/shared/RaceProgressArcRow.tsx` owns how it LOOKS, and takes plain props **specifically so it can be rendered without auth**. ⚠️ **That last point is the actual root cause, not the missing code.** The card fetches a tier-gated endpoint, so there was no way for anyone to LOOK at this feature short of a live paid session on a device — which is why a comment describing work that did not exist survived a review, a typecheck and a green suite. `/coach-preview` now renders all seven states (improving, gone backwards, goal beaten, holding, finish-goal two-point, week-one two-point, now-only) from fixtures, and 404s in production. **§109 still holds:** every point is a fact the runner already owns. A goal is a decision, not a prediction. No projected race-day finish, no rising line toward a date (§44.1); `raceProjectionHonesty.test.ts` still passes and caught the word "forecast" in a code comment on the way through. |

| No number reaches a runner that a human could not have produced (TREND-HR-PLAUSIBLE-01, UX-COACH-01) | PAID | 2026-09-12 | 🔴 **The Coach screen told the founder his easy runs averaged "77 (Apr avg) → 146 (now)", and Kit repeated it as established fact: *"Easy is costing you more than it did."*** An easy-run average of 77 bpm is not a training heart rate at any fitness level. **The trend maths was correct the entire way** — it was faithfully averaging a corrupt `strava_activities.avg_hr` row, and nothing between the row and the sentence objected. `isPlausibleRunHr` + `meanHr` in `runHistory.ts` are now the single gate on **every** HR average this app computes (the two averaging sites had written the same filter expression out by hand, and neither checked anything). **A bad row loses its HR, not its month** — one corrupt activity should cost that activity, not the bucket — and it still counts toward the run total, because the distance was never in doubt. A bucket with nothing usable returns **null, never 0** (`sessionKm` rule, SESSION-KM-01), so the trend declines to claim rather than claiming from zero. Bounds are 90–220 bpm in `RUN_HR_PLAUSIBLE`: not a coaching choice and not tunable, deliberately well below the ~110 an easy run actually reaches, so it only ever removes rows that are **wrong**, never rows that are merely low. Also retires `first.avgHr ?? 0` at the Coach fetch — the `distance_km ?? 0` family — which would have rendered "0 (Apr avg) → 146 (now)" and had Kit narrate a 146-bpm collapse. 17 tests, falsification-proved by restoring the ungated filter (5 go red). |

One value, two units, one file. **(2) The sheet hardcoded "Aerobic trend" and "long runs"** — harmless until the long-run card was retired the day before, after which **every** runner opening the sheet from the EASY RUN TREND card was told it was about long runs. **(3) The skeleton and pending states carried no label at all**, so the card read "Aerobic trend" while loading and flipped to "Easy run trend" when data landed. | Clock times and elapsed deltas have one owner (ADR-015) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-12 | `formatTime(totalSeconds)` existed **byte-identical in two files** — `app/api/race-times/route.ts` and `components/shared/RaceTimesCard.tsx` — the route formatting the projections list and the card re-formatting the arc, with nothing to catch them drifting. Same shape as the `formatPace` duplicate retired earlier. Both are now `formatClockTime` in `lib/format.ts`, the ADR-015 owner, alongside `formatElapsedDelta` (lifted from the route's private `formatDelta`). `formatElapsedDelta` is **unsigned by design**: the caller owns direction and says it in a word or an arrow. | Interpretation-led screen — ui-patterns §32 | FREE | 2026-09-12 | The shape Coach was rebuilt to, written up as a reusable pattern: **one subject, stated as a sentence, with its evidence beneath it.** Kit's read is the hero and must be an *interpretation, not a metric* (Wood: a view of progress is the illusion-of-progress class unless it changes what the runner does on Tuesday). The word and the picture are ONE block, from ONE owner, so the sentence and the image cannot contradict each other. **Counting blocks is not the fix** — Coach had seven and the problem was never seven, it was seven things answering different questions at the same volume. The pattern forbids: an invented composite score (§108), a predicted future value (§109), a tile restating a chart beside it, and a second non-verbal encoding of what the colour and label already carry. **The empty state is designed first** (McMillan: a surface that only speaks when the arrow is up is marketing). |
| Delivered volume caps gain an absolute-km floor (CHARITY-CAP-ABSFLOOR-01, §90/§94) | FREE | 2026-09-13 | Coaching Board CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT (batch sitting, `docs/decisions/coaching-board-2026-09-13-batch.md` §1). §90's injury delivered cap and §94's healthy delivered ramp are pure percentages, so on a LOW base a clinically trivial rise trips them — a knee-history masters 10K runner adding **+3 km** of non-long-run volume flagged at **+38%**, one short easy run. `DELIVERED_ABSOLUTE_FLOOR_KM = 3` now sits beneath the %: a warn fires only when the week-on-week rise clears BOTH the % cap AND 3 km. Floor set on measurement, not taste — a 2,790-plan injury/low-volume grid put the flagged non-long-run rises at **1–7 km (median 4)**; 3 km silences the ≤3 km arithmetic noise (**49%** of flags) while keeping **every rise ≥3 km**, so no real low-base spike is masked (Sims' condition binding — a 5 km floor was rejected for masking the 4–7 km band; Willy: a warn that fires on trivial rises is noise, and noise gets ignored, which is how a real breach is missed). **Gates the checker's warn, never the engine's trim** — the producer still caps to §12. Three artifacts one commit (`6b47211`): §90/§94 amendments, the constant, both invariants gated + `plan-invariants.md` rows. Sweep INJURY-CAP-DELIVERED 23.8% → 15.2% (both still wakeable), 1662 tests, matrix 17/17, 0 violations. |
| Race projections: the arc leads, the table is one tap away, and an ultra is not sold a marathon (UX-COACH-01 polish) | PAID | 2026-09-13 | A Coach design pass on the founder's bar (*"logical, premium, make me feel like it understands me"*). **(1) Progressive disclosure.** The `was · now · goal` arc (the runner's OWN race trajectory) is the hero; the 5K/10K/HM/Marathon reference table now **collapses behind a tap** ("Estimated times at other distances", ▾/▴, default closed) when the arc is present. This stops the card reading as a calculator and removes the duplication where the race-distance row echoes the arc's "now". Without an arc (benchmark/result variants, or no target race) the table is the content and stays open. **(2) An ultra is framed honestly.** VDOT cannot project past the marathon, so an ultra's arc drops to the nearest standard distance — and the box no longer headlines a **marathon time under "Your race"** (the one genuinely "this doesn't get me" moment, on the founder's own 100 km plan). Eyebrow → "Aerobic fitness", sub-line → "Marathon-equivalent", the race name moves into the honest caveat below the arc. Non-ultra unchanged. **(3) Load ratio speaks plainly** — "overloading/underloaded/balanced" → "above/under/right on your recent normal", one owner (`loadRatioContext`) feeding both the tile and the sheet; the number and its severity colour are unchanged. **No SLT:** presentation of an existing PAID feature — no change to what's collected, the tier line, or the audience. All new copy centralised in `raceProjectionsCopy.ts`, so `raceProjectionHonesty.test.ts` still guarantees no forward-looking claim. tsc + build clean, 25 race-projection tests green. `screen-architecture.md` also corrected (it still listed race projections as NOT belonging on Coach, contradicting the 2026-09-12 move). Residual: Coach is auth-gated, so the in-card visual is a device eyeball; the arc row itself renders at `/coach-preview`. |

| coaching-guard covers Bash (doctrine edits made through the shell) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-13 | The PreToolUse coaching guard matched `Edit\|Write\|MultiEdit\|NotebookEdit` only, so every doctrine edit made through Bash — `sed -i`, `cat > file`, a `python3 - <<'PY'` heredoc — passed with no guard, no prompt and no trace. ADR-017 and CLAUDE.md both claim convening is automatic and must not rely on remembering; that was **false for the path a Bash-preferring session uses for everything**. For Bash the guard reads `command`, not `file_path`, and a doctrine path is necessary but NOT sufficient: it also requires a write signal (redirect **target**, `sed -i`/`tee`/`patch`, `cp`/`mv` **destination**, or an interpreter plus a write-mode token). Reads win ties deliberately — `sed -n '1,40p'` and `grep` on a doctrine file run dozens of times a session, and a guard that fires on those gets switched off, which this repo records as equivalent to having no guard. Tests 26 → **50 cases**, both directions, including `grep DOC > /tmp/out` (reads doctrine, writes elsewhere → silent) and `cp DOC /tmp/backup` (copies FROM → silent). A Monday race has no in-week day before it, so race week holds only the race — **the configuration almost no real runner has, and the one every check was green on**. That is why the race-eve defect survived. The six canonical cases now end on the Sunday of their final week, `charityRaceDate` returns the Sunday, and a new case **`07-hm-monday-race`** keeps the early-week edge visible until PV2-G is built — moving everything to Sunday without it would have turned a known open defect green. Two boundary personas bumped a week rather than silently becoming refusal cases. The cohort grid is deliberately left on Mondays: re-baselining parity is its own declared move. |
| LR-SHORTFALL-DURATION-01 — long-run shortfall measured on the session's own axis (§66 Amendment 1) | FREE | 2026-09-14 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-14.** §66's shortfall trigger filtered on planned DISTANCE, so duration-anchored long runs (beginners: 95.8% of sessions) were dropped — **2,547 of 7,965 long runs (32.0%), and the trigger completely dead on 153 of 621 plans (24.6%)**. A second gate meant even a firing trigger would have no-opped. Upstream, the same expression left `planned_load_km` null, so the post-run card read **"No distance data."** after every run for that cohort. **The filed premise was wrong**: §66 bullet 3 excludes duration-primary long runs explicitly, so this was a ratified scope decision, not a bug — the board amended it because §80 (written later) makes time on feet the prescription. **Deriving km was rejected**: it recovers 100% of the dropped sessions and would report a walk-breaking first-timer short against a number they were never given. Moving time, not elapsed (walking registers as movement). 82% across both axes, no new numeric. Trim in minutes, never a new `distance_km`. **Reach 68.0% → 100.0%, dead-trigger plans 24.6% → 0.0%; parity IDENTICAL across 5,832 cases.** `INV-PLAN-LONG-RUN-HAS-AN-AXIS` guards the precondition; 23 trigger cases incl. 14 pre-existing as regression. |
| CONFIG-CONSUMER-01 — every config surface must have a consumer, not just a principle | FREE | 2026-09-14 | `configConsumer.test.ts` proved that every `GENERATION_CONFIG` and `PLAN_SIGNATURES` key has a reader — and covered **none of the surfaces the 2026-09-13 orphans actually lived on** (`intensity_zones`/`fuel_every_mins` are catalogue ROW fields; `ZONE_DISCIPLINE_BANDS` is in `lib/coaching/constants.ts`). Extended to `SESSION_FORMAT`, session-catalogue row fields and the coaching constants, with §17's authority/declarative/superseded/debt sort and registers that only shrink. **Found three live defects:** `HR_ZONE_TOLERANCE_BPM` declared 3 and read by nothing while `zoneRules` hardcoded its own `tolerance = 2` for the same job (wired at 2, the shipped value — changing it would move `hr_in_zone_pct`); `race_pace_distances` declared and unread → RACE-PACE-OVERLAY-REACH-01; and `SIG_SUPERSEDED` citing §24d (finish-goal 5K/10K) for the HM race-pace long run, so a field left the debt register on a principle that does not cover it. The checker shipped two bugs of its own, both now tested: excluding the declaring MODULE reported `quality_warmup_min_mins` dead though `sessionSplit` reads it everywhere, and the narrowed version went **vacuous** on the catalogue because the row INTERFACE declares every field. Residual stated: substring-based, so a key read only by a dead function counts as consumed — `ZONE_DISCIPLINE_BANDS` still passes. 16 cases. |
| Race-pace segment dose has one owner and a check (§25 Amendment 1, LR-RACE-SEGMENT-PCT-01) | FREE | 2026-09-14 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-14, verification sitting** — run after the founder asked whether a coaching review had happened. It had not: the RACE-PACE-OVERLAY-REACH-01 sitting was conducted inline and **skipped the mandatory conflict scan**, which immediately surfaced **§25** (*"the final 25–40% of the long run"*). Three answers were live for one segment on a 165-min marathon long run: the row's `race_pace_pct: 40` (66 min), a hand-typed note saying *"Final 30–50% at MP"* (**above §25's own ceiling**), and the card applying §16's general 20% to the MAIN set (27 min, **16% of the run**). **Two rendered on the same card**, and the HM contradiction was NEW — introduced that morning. `race_pace_pct` restored as the **single owner** (HM 35, MARATHON 40), read by note and card; the percentage is of the LONG RUN so the parts now sum to the session; both hand-typed note strings deleted. Willy declined to veto but recorded a guard (the dose scales: 76 min at MP on a 191-min run; an absolute ceiling would be new doctrine). **Declared parity move: 540/5,832 cases — HM 324, MARATHON 216, time_target only; 5K/10K/50K/100K all 0**, delta is the two note strings. `INV-PLAN-LR-RACE-SEGMENT-PCT` reads the note's own stated number, proven wakeable. 14 cases. **The transferable lesson: an unread number is not automatically a wrong one.** |
