Guards measured, not reasoned: §47 step-back 30→0, §24b three-part 396→0, §24e ultra 342→0. After: HM 18/18. `verify:parity` IDENTICAL across 5,832 cases — generation unaffected. `racePaceOverlay.test.ts`, 11 cases. |
| A safety cap is never exempted, only downgraded (§52 Amendment 1, MAINT-EXEMPT-SCOPE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-13 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-13.** `INV-PLAN-LR-MAX-WEEKLY-PCT` (§52's 60% long-run-share cap) was switched OFF for any plan classified `maintenance`, justified as "already surfaced in volume_constraint_note" — but that note explains low TOTAL volume and says nothing about lopsidedness. **51% of plans classify maintenance, so the cap went unchecked on half of them.** Measured with the exemption removed: **268 of 6,588 weeks breach, every one in a maintenance plan and none in a build plan**; 60 of 314 maintenance plans carry at least one; worst is a **beginner marathon plan with a 26.0 km long run in a 34 km week (76% of the week in one session)**. Now `warn` for maintenance, `error` for build — §34's honest-residual pattern, not a hard error, because 60 plans would stop generating and the runner's volume constraint is real. Declared rate 8.6% (1368/15973) where it previously reported nothing. The four other maintenance exemptions were examined and left alone: §24/§23/§46 are **circular** exemptions (the plan is maintenance BECAUSE it failed them), and the test is whether an exemption is circular or merely convenient. `lopsidedWeek.test.ts`, 6 cases. |

**Monetisation model**: Hybrid Reverse Trial — 14 days full (PAID) access for all new users, then graceful downgrade to FREE. See `docs/canonical/monetisation-strategy.md`.

**Rule**: FREE tier must not expose paid-tier data or imply its existence without an explicit product decision.

**Rule**: All AI calls (Anthropic API) route through Next.js API routes only — never client-side. Rule-based engine makes zero AI calls — enforced at route level.
| A recovery week must not sit at phase position 2 (§95 Amendment 1, DELOAD-POS2-01) | FREE | 2026-09-15 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-15.** §95 declared the rule on 2026-09-07 and shipped only a CHECK — `computeDeloadWeeks` knew nothing about position 2, so the defect was masked, not repaired, and fired on **16.1% of the swept population (2,570/15,973)**. A first build was reverted on 2026-09-14 after flipping **453 plans to maintenance**; root cause now measured: its guard `since === recoveryFreq - 3` **degenerates to `since === 0` at the masters cadence of 3** (`RECOVERY_WEEK_FREQUENCY_MASTERS`), placing a deload the week immediately after one just placed (`[3,6] → [3,4,7]`) — adjacent recovery weeks, inflated count, crushed peak. `since >= 1` is the whole fix. **§95 is a PREFERENCE and now YIELDS** to any ratified ERROR the §87 placement does not also carry, because re-locating a deload changes session COMPOSITION, not just placement (marathon `[4,8,12] → [2,6,10]` is **+1 hard / −3 running**, taking §1 to **19.6% against an 18% ceiling**); recorded as `meta.deload_position2_yielded`. **Adjacency and a lengthened loading run are HARD** and never traded (Willy: back-to-back deloads remove a loading stimulus rather than adding recovery; Sims: masters are the slowest-recovering population). Measured: sweep firing **16.1% → 0.2%**, standard runners (freq 4) **21.7% → 0.0%**, adjacency **0**, `cohortShape` **unchanged** — no plan reclassified, which is the difference from the reverted build. Masters residual 52.2% is the **provably unsatisfiable** set: brute force over every legal placement shows the full constraint set is unsatisfiable on **1,944 of 3,726 masters plans** and **0 of 3,726** standard (D-21). `deloadCadence.ts` (`avoidPosition2`), yield in `generateRulePlan`; `deloadCadence.test.ts` +4 cases, adjacency pin falsification-verified against the reverted regression. |
| Cohort grid varies `recent_quality_training`, unlocking §89 (GRID-COVERAGE-02 Phase 1) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-15 | **Direct follow-on to GRID-COVERAGE-01.** `cohortGrid` set `recent_quality_training` to the constant `'occasional'` — and it is the gate **ADR-021/§89** keys on, so `early_quality_onset` fired on **0 of 7,452 plans**. §89's experience-gated onset, §97's one-week on-ramp and the `oneWeekOnRamp` arm of §79's intensity re-entry were therefore **unreachable by every check built on this grid**: `cohort:shape`, the invariant-liveness corpus, and every `measure-*` script. A green run over a constant axis is not coverage. Added as a **2-value** axis (`occasional`/`regular`; `none` behaves identically for every mechanism that reads the field, so a third value would double runtime to prove a distinction the engine does not draw) — 7,776 → 15,552 inputs, ~16s. **Measured: `early_quality_onset` 0 → 984 plans (6.6%); `intensity_reentry_active` 1,776 → 4,536.** Declared cohort re-baseline (`constrainedByInputs` −0.9pp, `volumeConstrained` −0.9pp, `constraintNote` −0.7pp, 5K maintenance −0.6pp — all the `'regular'` half getting earlier onset). Also adds the **coverage GATE** the item asked for: `cohortShape.test.ts` fails the build if a declared axis never varies, with deliberate exclusions listed (the SWEEP-BASELINE-01 debt-register pattern) — the gap that let this drift. **Phase 2 remains in the backlog:** `injury_history`, `user_declared_level`, `weeks_at_current_volume`, `day_budgets`, `foundation_decision` are still unset, and runtime is the binding constraint. |
| §79 intensity re-entry window has one owner (INTENSITY-REENTRY-OWNER-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-15 | **Zero behaviour delta, proven before anything else changed.** The §79 withhold predicate was hand-written **twice inside `generateRulePlan`, twenty lines apart, with different loop variables** — `intensityReentryActive && wn <= intensityReentryWeeks` in the build-slot reservation and `... && weekN <= ...` in the per-week selector gate. Same fact, two producers, agreeing only by being adjacent: the **DELOAD-OWNER-01 fault exactly**, and feeding the two channels different predicates is what measured **47 → 594** sweep failures when they were changed out of step. Extracted to `lib/plan/intensityReentry.ts` owning the window's `active`, `weeks` and both readings (`withheldIn` calendar, `withheldAtQualityIndex` onset-anchored). Preserves §97's ordering trap explicitly — the one-week-on-ramp arm is tested FIRST and takes the LONGER window, because a gated runner is `tissueConditioned` by construction and the other order silently discards Willy's §97 condition. `intensityReentry.test.ts`, 12 cases, with the same mechanical ownership guard as `deloadCadence.test.ts` (falsification-verified: re-introducing the raw expression anywhere in `lib/` turns it red). Proven byte-equivalent by the full sweep and `cohortShape` before the semantic work began. |
| A returning runner's quality leads with tempo, not intervals (§79 Amendment 1, QUALITY-ONSET-ORDER-01) | FREE | 2026-09-15 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-14 (finding) + 2026-09-15 (mechanism).** §79's re-entry window is an ORDERING claim — *"withhold VO2max/hills for the opening weeks so quality LEADS with tempo/threshold"* — encoded against **CALENDAR** weeks. `plannedQuality` is 0 for every base week and every deload week, so weeks 1..N are all-easy by construction and there was nothing there to withhold: **the window closed the week before quality began.** Measured before: of 48 plans with re-entry ACTIVE, the first quality session fell inside the protective window in **0 of 48**, and was Zone 4-5 anyway in 32 of 48. Now counts **quality-carrying** weeks via `intensityReentry.ts`'s `withheldAtQualityIndex`, asked by both consumers with the counter they already shared (the build-slot IIFE's rotation index and the driver loop's `buildRotationIndex` walk the identical sequence) — the filed "single precomputed `Set`" design was unnecessary and would have added a THIRD producer. **Measured after: returning runners whose FIRST quality session is VO2max 25.4% → 16.8%.** ⚠️ This is what makes §5's adaptation-window swap fire at all (0 → 576 plans), so it ships with §22 Amendment and V2-SWAP-RESIZE-01. |
| Omitting VO2max for a returning runner is a declared decision (§79 Amendment 2, REENTRY-DEPTH-01) | FREE | 2026-09-15 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-15.** Filed as "recalibrate the depth numeric"; the measurement said that was the wrong question. Depths **1, 2 and 3 are identical and change nothing** — only 4 bites, so recalibrating down is turning the rule off while appearing to tune it. And of the plans where the quality-week reading changes the opening stimulus, **576 lost VO2max ENTIRELY and 0 were merely re-ordered**: the window only WITHHOLDS, carries no obligation to place the stimulus afterwards, and §53's rotation fills the freed slots with other categories. **Withholding is not deferral.** Board ruled omission LEGITIMATE on §5's own authority, which records Seiler verbatim — *"either commit to it properly in the build, or do not do it. The middle position is the only indefensible one"* — and **REJECTED** requiring deferral, because manufacturing a late VO2max block is exactly that indefensible middle. What is forbidden is the silent version (§87/§95: *a defensible outcome reached at random is a coincidence, not a decision*). `meta.intensity_reentry_omission_note` is stamped on **3,384 plans** and rendered through the ONE note renderer (`planRationaleNotes`, PLAN-NOTE-SURFACE-01). `INV-PLAN-REENTRY-OMISSION-DECLARED` (error) derives from the **placed sessions**, never a meta flag, so the producer cannot satisfy it by asserting it behaved; falsification-verified — suppressing the note drops generation by exactly 3,384 plans. ⚠️ **NOT ratified:** the pre-existing 49.7% no-VO2max / 74.8% no-hill-reps baseline on returning runners is filed separately as REENTRY-VO2MAX-BASELINE-01. |
| A session displaced by §5's adaptation window is exempt from §22 (§22 Amendment, V2-SWAP-S22-01) | FREE | 2026-09-15 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-15.** Repairing §79 makes `applyV2Vo2MaxOnsetTiming` fire for the first time; it displaces a threshold session into the week that held the first VO2max, and on a time-targeted plan that week is §22-governed. §22 **exempts VO2max** (`isVo2maxSession`) but not threshold, so a week legal BY EXEMPTION became illegal — **288 ERROR violations**, completely homogeneous (144× 5K + 144× 10K, all time-targeted, all `intensity_reentry_active`, all `recent_quality_training: 'regular'`), visible only because GRID-COVERAGE-02 Phase 1 started varying that field the same morning. `ruleEngine.ts` had documented that late swapping "breaks §22" and nothing enforced it. Now a fourth exemption on the footing of the three §22 already grants (VO2max, effort-governed §40b, mixed-anchor §85) with their identical justification: the plan-level `INV-PLAN-RACE-SPECIFIC-EXPOSURE-RATIO` still holds the plan to a race-pace share. **Binding condition MEASURED: across 576 plans carrying a displaced session, ratio violations 0 and per-week §22 violations 0.** Stamp is STRUCTURAL (`displaced_by_adaptation_window`), unreachable from `EnrichedWeekSchema` — a label test would be rewritten by the AI voice pass (D-17). **Rejected:** §79 yielding (reinstates the Zone 4-5-first defect), §5 yielding (Willy — compresses the dose into fewer weeks against the taper), and swapping with a race-pace partner (measured insufficient: only 144 of 288 failing plans have one). |
| A relocated session is re-sized for the phase it lands in (V2-SWAP-RESIZE-01) | FREE | 2026-09-15 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-15.** Filed as COHERENCE-SELECT-01 — *"§53 selection has no volume/structure-coherence guard"* — which was the **second wrong diagnosis** on this item. Real cause: `applyV2Vo2MaxOnsetTiming` physically swaps two Session OBJECTS between weeks and updated **only `session.id`**. `progressive_tempo` is sized from `PROGRESSIVE_TEMPO_MAIN_MINS[fitness][phase]` (intermediate: build 24 / peak 28 / taper 20), so a session sized for BUILD (24 min main, `derived_set` 3×8 min, `duration_mins` 43) landed in a PEAK week needing 28/~48 and `INV-PLAN-STRUCTURED-SESSION-DURATION-COHERENT` (§8) fired — **64 failures in a 2,916-input probe**, all `progressive_tempo`, concentrated at low volume (km=15: 16 · km=25: 8 · km=40: 0 · km=60: 0) because pool thinness makes it the displaced row. The session was **not internally incoherent** (24 min of work genuinely fits 43 min); it carried the wrong dose for its new block. Re-size runs through `makeQualitySession` — **the same sizer the constructor uses**, inheriting its floor protections rather than a bespoke path (Willy/Sims) — adopting only the phase-dependent dose, leaving identity, label, notes and the §1/Q2 cue untouched. Probe **64 → 0**. Now load-bearing on **576 plans** (it was unreachable until §79 was repaired). |

Also added the two note-invariant mutations the liveness harness then demanded (`INV-PLAN-RETURNING-RUNNER-NOTE-PRESENT`, `INV-PLAN-REENTRY-OMISSION-DECLARED`) rather than baselining them as debt. |

| Plan rules report — the four named rules, in words (PLAN-RULES-REPORT-01) | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-15 | `npm run verify` answers "did anything break?" in the engine's vocabulary — invariant codes, sweep counts, cohort rates. (2) It asserted "every wizard variant must change the plan" and flagged three that are identical **by design** — declaring a level you have already been assessed is a no-op (§79), and adding `knee` to a runner who already carries `shin_splints` changes nothing because ADR-022 treats them as one volume-capped class. | §47 peak long-run alternation counts PAIRS, not positions | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-16 | Latent defect found by the sweep while shipping LONG-RUNWAY-EARNS-PLAN-01: **9 new `INV-PLAN-PEAK-LR-ALTERNATION` errors**, every one carrying §47's EXCEPTION preconditions (`hard_session_relationship: 'love'` + `training_age: '5yr+'` + no injury). The exception permits **one** back-to-back peak set. `applyPeakLongRunAlternation` walked `offset % 2`: on a two-week peak phase, consuming the exception at the single odd offset produced exactly one adjacent pair; on a **three-week** peak phase it skipped the only step-back the parity scheduled, leaving all three weeks peak-level and therefore **two** pairs. **The checker counted PAIRS, the producer counted POSITIONS, and they agreed only while the phase was two weeks long.** The defect predates the plan-length change; the change made three-week peak phases reachable at HM. The producer now steps a week back when the week AFTER it is peak-level — the same question the invariant asks, so there is no parity left to disagree about. |

From the next run a clean audit would have surfaced as a constitutional finding, with every useful field reading `-` because Q4 selects `codes`/`reason`/`outcome`, not the summary's payload. That is precisely the *"reports an already-fixed problem in the present tense trains Russ to ignore it"* failure the prompt warns about three paragraphs later. **Routine `trig_01P5snwo2k4reDGrX4Z3wkyC` (Zonna daily ops digest, 08:30 Europe/London) updated via RemoteTrigger:** Q4 now excludes the summary row; new **Q4B** selects it and reads `checked` / `invalid` / `newest_invalid_plan_age_days` / `invalid_by_plan_age`; STEP 2B gains the interpretation, and STEP 4 renders it in the dashboard's Engine-health section. **The interpretation is the point:** judge on the AGE of the newest breaching plan, never the raw `invalid` count — old plans stay on the fleet under old engine rules, so the count drifts upward forever and is not a signal. A MISSING Q4B row is itself the finding (the audit did not run, and Q4's silence then means nothing). Audit runs 07:45 UTC (`ops-cron-plan-audit.yml`), digest 08:30 London, so the event is ~45 min old when read. Original prompt backed up before the edit; name/cron/enabled/model unchanged, verified by diff — the only delta beyond the drafted change was a deliberate STEP 4 strengthening. |

Willy's objection filed as `LR-ABS-CAP-LOWVOL-01` and deliberately NOT taken the day before the charity showcase — it changes what the engine prescribes. | LR-ABS-CAP-LOWVOL-01 — §45's +5km long-run step tapers on a small long run (§45 Am.2) | FREE | 2026-09-17 | §9 sizes the long run as a share of weekly volume; the `+5km` absolute step allowance was **the one part of long-run prescription that ignored the week entirely** — +17% on a 30km long run, **+83% on a 6km one, arriving in a single week, on the least-conditioned runner in the population.** Now `max(prevLR × 20%, min(+5km, prevLR × 50%))`. **The CONTROL GROUP is what settled it:** on the low-volume cohort 32.5% of absolute-arm steps were ≥+40% in one week against **2.7%** in the control. ⚠️ Frequency was never the tell — the arm binds MORE often on ordinary runners (37.8% vs 29.2%) — **magnitude is, by 12×**, and a whole-population measurement would have shown nothing wrong. That is why the first sitting (2026-09-16) ruled INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE. **50% is derived, not picked:** §9 puts the build-phase long run at 30% of the week, so the board's "15% of the week" IS 50% of the long run. ⚠️ **The basis changed during implementation:** built on `prev.weekly_km` it broke **140 plans, which threw** — §45 runs mid-pipeline and the long run is re-anchored afterwards, so producer and checker read different weekly volumes (the LR-CAP-BLIND-01 shape: one bug, two copies). Re-expressed on `prevLR`, which the % arm already reads, the two cannot drift — **and the result is strictly better than the version the board costed** (peak LR falls 12.5% not 22.7%; 7 plans lose their time goal not 18; worst jump 83%→51% not 57%). Cost accepted with open eyes: 7 of 1,149 plans (0.6%) can no longer reach §24's specificity floor and reclassify maintenance-grade, keeping the honest `volume_constraint_note` (McMillan's condition). Median plan untouched. **Board withheld ship timing; founder elected to ship the day before the charity showcase.** verify exit 0 (2,052 tests / 222 files) · `cohort:shape` 4 metrics moved, all declared · `verify:parity` 1,085/5,940 (18.3%) with **0 of 1,944 changed at 55km/wk**. Record: `docs/decisions/coaching-board-2026-09-17-lr-abs-cap.md`. |
| PLAN-FITNESS-01 — injury-history runners build again, and the long run reaches its floor (§2 Am.3, §24/§80 Am.) | FREE | 2026-09-17 | **Two defects that made plans not fit for purpose, found by asking a question no check asked: does this plan BUILD the runner, and far enough to finish safely?** **D1:** isolated by toggling one flag — same runner, 18 weeks, everything identical but a knee history: net build **+91% healthy vs +6% injured** (standard cadence), **+79% vs +3%** (masters); 28.9% and 66.7% never built at all. A knee-history runner reached a marathon start line never having run beyond 14 km. Amendment 1 capped the post-deload bounceback at 5% while a deload cuts 15%, so recovery takes 7 weeks and the next deload arrives in 3. Amendment 2 (2026-09-16) found the ratchet and raised the deload to 85% — **short of its own break-even, 86.4% standard and 90.7% masters.** ⚠️ The injury × masters cell **existed in no grid** and is the worst case. **D2:** M1 — a first marathon with a **24-week runway, no injury, no time cap — peaked at a 21 km long run, 50% of race distance.** §24's and §80's specificity floors were gated on `phase === 'peak'`, so §80's 29.5 km ask arrived with 2-3 weeks left and §45's +5 km/week could not climb to it. ⚠️ **Not §52, not §9** — the long run tracked §9's share for 14 of 19 weeks because nothing else asked. **Willy reversed his own RAMP-BOUNCEBACK-01 veto on the evidence**: he rejected the return when a 70% cut made it +43%; Amendment 2's shallower cut makes it +17.6%. His condition is mechanical — `INJURY_BOUNCEBACK_MIN_DELOAD_PCT` gates the exemption on the constant, so a deeper cut withdraws it automatically. **Results:** injury net build 18.2% → **45.5%** (standard) and 0% → **31.3%** (masters); never-builds **28.9% → 0%** and **66.7% → 0%**; healthy runners unchanged. Marathon peak long run: M1 21 → **26 km**, M2 25 → **29.5**, M3 14 → **26**, M5 27.5 → **29.5**, M1d 19 → **23.5**. Long-run compounding **improved** (plans with a 2-wk window >40% 28.4% → 23.0%). ⚠️ **Costs stated:** `verify:parity` **3,310/5,940 changed (55.7%)** — the largest single change this engine has had; deviation scan **MED 23 → 25** (both new ones "peak long run far beyond proven distance", the honest trade, HIGH still 0); M1 still reaches 26 km against a 30-32 km norm. ⚠️ **Three earlier diagnoses were wrong and are recorded in the decision file** (long run as cause, back-loaded curve, post-pass caps) — each presented before being tested. Artifacts: §2 Am.3 · §24/§80 Am. · `INJURY_BOUNCEBACK_MIN_DELOAD_PCT` · `SPECIFICITY_RAMP_START_PCT` · `INV-PLAN-BOUNCEBACK-BOUNDED` `>=`→`>` · `injuryBouncebackBuilds.test.ts` · **`scripts/measure-plan-fitness.ts`, a committed before/after harness**. verify exit 0, 2,057 tests / 223 files. Record: `docs/decisions/coaching-board-2026-09-17-plan-fitness.md`. |
| LR-DELOAD-CUT-01 — a deload no longer cuts the long run harder than the week (§3 Am.) | FREE | 2026-09-17 | §3 says volume drops to 70% **of the week**. Nothing asks the long run to be cut harder — and it was, on **50.4% of 2,817 deload weeks** (week cut median 22%, long run median 30%). Worst traced: a week falling 44 → 43km (−2%) while its long run fell 20.5 → 13.5km (−34%). The long run then had to climb all the way back and ran out of weeks — **the single root cause of every marathon shortfall left after PLAN-FITNESS-01.** ⚠️ **Mechanism established only after TWO hypotheses were disproved.** Not the specificity pull switching off (the gap is identical in base phase, where no pull exists); not unplaced sessions (placed easy count equals planned on 100% of weeks). It is: **the long run hits §9's share of the PLANNED week while the rest of the week is trimmed in placement (median −6km, p10 −22km)** — same numerator, smaller denominator, so the delivered share runs 37–42% against §9's 28–30%. A deload's smaller planned week takes the trim proportionally harder and the share falls back to 33–34%. That asymmetry IS the disproportionate cut. **Fixes:** marathon finish knee+45+ **26.0 → 29.5km** (meets §80's floor), time-goal knee **30.5 → 32.0km**, time-goal knee+45+ 26.0 → 30.5, M3 persona 26.0 → 29.5km with net build 54% → 79%. ⚠️ **SAFER, not merely longer** — the point Willy's veto of the resume approach turned on: on the low-base beginner the deload cut goes **−61% → −34%** and the worst single-week jump **+50% → +47%**. Cutting less means climbing less. Population-wide, plans carrying a 2-week long-run rise >+40% fall **23.0% → 19.3%**. ⚠️ **`INV-PLAN-LONG-CAP-MINS` caught a real bug in the first build** — raising the deload long run ignored §9's absolute minutes cap and threw on 48 grid plans (a 5K long run at 92 min vs a 90-min ceiling). `applyLongRunCap` reused as single owner. ⚠️ **COST, ruled by the founder:** marathon plans classified `maintenance` rise **68% → 72.7% (+4.7pp)**, driven entirely by §52 lopsidedness (88 → 106 in a 753-plan sample), **not** floor failures (293 → 292). Willy's §52 bound caps the deload week; the lopsidedness arises in the weeks after it — **the second time that pattern has appeared.** Accepted because those weeks genuinely are long-run-dominated and §52 saying so is correct, against leaving knee-history and masters runners 3.5–5.7km short. verify exit 0, 2,061 tests / 225 files, 0 hard failures; `verify:parity` 1,144/5,940 (19.3%); cohort:shape 6 metrics re-baselined. Record: `docs/decisions/coaching-board-2026-09-17-plan-fitness.md`. |
| PLAN-FITNESS-GATE-01 — the fitness harness is gated on every build, not run when remembered | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-17 | `measure:fitness` answers the FOURTH question — *does this plan actually BUILD the runner, and far enough to finish the race safely?* `verify` proves plans are VALID, `verify:parity` that they are UNCHANGED, `cohort:shape` that the POPULATION has not shifted. **None can see a plan that is valid, unchanged, correctly classified and useless** — which is exactly what an injury-history runner was getting: eighteen weeks ending on the weekly volume they started. ⚠️ **The harness was NOT gated for the first hours of its life.** It ran when someone typed it, which this repo's own history (liveness debt, decorative config, an eslint rule installed but never configured) says is the same as not running. Now `lib/plan/planFitness.test.ts` inside `npm run verify`: **`neverBuildsPct` rising above zero fails with NO tolerance** (a plan that does not train the runner is not a plan); build regressions fail at 5pp, marathon long-run specificity at 3pp, and the 11 charity personas are named individually so a failure says WHO. **Falsified before shipping** — reverting §3 Am. makes 3 of the 5 assertions fire and name the affected cohorts and personas. Baseline `planFitnessBaseline.json`, re-baselined only with a declared reason via `-- --write`, the same contract as `cohort:shape`. Runs in 3.8s. Also wired into the `coaching-board` skill: a ruling that changes what the engine PRESCRIBES must carry these numbers, because a ruling without them is a ruling about how a plan is CLASSIFIED, not whether it works. verify exit 0, 2,066 tests / 226 files. |

It changes **nothing the engine prescribes** — its only consumers are two prompts and the `plan.meta.athlete` stamp. '?'` guard never fired and `''.split(' ')` walked to `''`. ⚠️ **Silent, as this repo's bugs always are** — no crash, no log. `lib/profileInitials.ts` is now the single owner (profile name → plan name → email → `'?'`) and `profileInitials.test.ts` **was proved RED against the old implementation before being trusted green** (3 of 8 fail). Two latent instances of the same class fixed in passing: `postRaceReshape.ts` was addressing nobody, and the profile `<label>`s were decorative text with no `htmlFor`, so screen readers read the fields as unlabelled. `IdentityCard` + `ProfileSection` lifted out of the 12k-line `DashboardClient` so **`/me-preview` can render five states** — the harness is the point, because both sit behind auth, a plan and a tab, which is exactly how a hardcoded placeholder reached production. | TT-STRUCTURE-01 — a 5K time trial shows the 5 km, and agrees with its own coach note | FREE | 2026-09-17 | Founder-reported. The §78 benchmark rendered **"warm-up ~3km · main set ~2km · cool-down ~0km"** beside a Kit note reading *"warm up easy for 10 minutes, then 5 km as hard as you can hold"* — **the measurement the whole recalibration feature depends on was shown as 2 km.** ⚠️ **One bug, not three: a producer/consumer disagreement about what a field MEANS.** `ruleEngine` builds the trial saying so in as many words — `distance_km` IS the measurement, `duration_mins` is *"the effort itself (warm-up/cool-down live in the note)"*. `sessionComposer` assumed the opposite (that `duration_mins` is the whole session), split it 15/main/5 via `sessionSplit()`, and `withDistances` stamped each part at `kmPerMin = 5/total` — so the warm-up ate 15 of the 29 minutes and 3 of the 5 km, and `apportionRoundedDistance` forced the remainder, rounding the cool-down to **~0km**. Reproduced from the pure functions before a line was changed, and again end-to-end on current `main`. **The time trial is now its own shape** (`shape: 'time_trial'`): the main set is the trial exactly (**no `~`** — every other figure on the card is an estimate, this one is the prescription), the bookends are minutes, and the session total is warm-up + trial + cool-down instead of the effort alone. ⚠️ **It is the one shape whose parts do NOT partition the total, so SESSION-RECONCILE-01's premise genuinely does not hold for it** — scoped out with the reasoning, NOT loosened, and given a **stricter** contract in its place (`TT-STRUCTURE-01`): main set == the trial, bookends in minutes, and **the card's warm-up minutes must equal the number the coach note promises**. That last assertion is the one that would have caught this; nothing checked prose against structure. **Zero new numerics and no doctrine numeric touched** — the warm-up reads `warmup_min_duration_mins`, which *is* the ten minutes the ratified note already promised, so the two can no longer drift. `isTimeTrial()` added to `sessionRole.ts` as the single owner of `type === 'hard'` (the only producer is `applyRecalibrationTimeTrial`); `enrich.ts` and two sites in `invariants.ts` had written it out by hand and now share it. Reads a field every existing plan already carries, so **plans generated before the fix render correctly** and parity is untouched by the display change. Falsified both ways: red on the old composer, and red again with the warm-up set to 15 (*"card says 15 min warm-up, note says 10"*) and the main set at 40% (*"main set 2km ≠ the 5km trial"*). verify exit 0, 2,081 tests / 226 files. |
| AI-PROVENANCE-01 — Kit stops being credited for copy no model wrote | FREE | 2026-09-17 | Founder-caught on the 5K time trial: the Session Detail card rendered **"Kit · YOUR COACH"** with the AI provenance rail over hand-authored `ruleEngine.ts` copy that `applyEnrichment` is *explicitly told to skip* — **Kit claiming authorship of the one string in the app that is most definitively not his.** CLAUDE.md states the rule plainly: *"Apply only to actual model output; never to rule-engine, hand-authored copy."* ⚠️ **The card decided provenance with `coach_notes.length > 0`** — which answers "are there notes", not "did a model write them", and **the rule engine writes `coach_notes` too**. **MEASURED: 12,972 of 31,517 running sessions on 563 FREE plans (41.2%) carry rule-engine notes, and every one rendered Kit's byline** — on plans that are never enriched by design (`meta.enrichment: 'skipped'`). Not a free-tier-only bug: it also hit the time trial on *every* paid plan, and every note on any plan where enrichment silently failed (ADR-006 makes that invisible). `lib/plan/notesProvenance.ts → sessionNotesAreAiAuthored()` is now the single owner, and the view never re-derives it. ⚠️ **The error is not symmetric, which decides every uncertain case:** crediting a model for human copy is a false claim, withholding credit is modesty, so **every unknown resolves to FALSE** — unknown enrichment state, legacy plan with no field, no notes, the §78 trial, or a week ENRICH-PARTIAL-01 reverted to rule copy. That last case needed a new `Week.enrichment_reverted` stamp: `applied_partial` was a plan-wide claim while `revertWeekCopy` was quietly handing specific weeks back to the engine, and nothing recorded it. **Set only during enrichment, so parity is untouched** (parity generates rule plans). Falsified: restoring the shipped `length > 0` test fails 6 of 8 assertions including the population one. verify exit 0, 2,089 tests / 227 files. |
| COPY-GLYPH-01 — the section symbol stops reaching runners, and a guard keeps it out | FREE | 2026-09-17 | Founder-reported, repeatedly: **`§45` was rendering inside a "Why this plan" tile** on the Plan screen (*"week-on-week long-run cap (§45) prevented reaching the ratio"*). Now **0.0%**. ⚠️ **The guard is the deliverable, not the one-line fix.** `emittedCopyGlyphs.test.ts` generates plans across the cohort grid and scans every runner-readable string (`meta.*`, week theme/label, session label/description/why/difficulty_note, coach_notes). **It reads EMITTED COPY, not source** — a source grep cannot tell a doctrine reference in a comment from one inside a runner-facing template, and cannot see a string assembled from parts at generation time. `lib/marketing/noEmDash.test.ts` covers marketing surfaces by reading files, which is why `brand.md` has carried *"Currently out of scope: the app"* as a known gap. This closes the mechanism half of **BRAND-EMDASH-01**, which had independently proposed exactly this. The em-dash arm ships as a **ratchet, not a pass**: em dashes are in **100% of plans** (26,727 session labels alone), so it asserts the debt does not GROW at 12 runner-facing fields while the founder decision stays open, and re-baselines DOWN only. ⚠️ **A scratch probe said 11 and was wrong** (it omitted `week.label`) — the baseline is read from the test's own failure message, never a side count. Golden snapshot moved one line on one plan; parity 320/5,940 (5.4%), and the source diff is a single string with everything else comments. verify exit 0, 2,091 tests / 228 files. |
| PLAN-NOTE-VOICE-01 — "Why this plan" stops being a wall and starts being coaching | FREE | 2026-09-17 | **SLT 2026-09-17**, convened after the founder read a real plan on device. Revision of PLAN-NOTE-SURFACE-01 (2026-09-09), which shipped with the registry note *"Not visually smoke-tested behind auth (trivial map over tested logic + proven components)"* — **it was never looked at, which is how 130 words of engine-voice reached the runner.** MEASURED across 563 plans: **91.1% saw a note, 74% saw two or three, mean 130 words, worst 254**; MAINTENANCE + VOLUME appeared together on 200 plans and **157 (78.5%) blamed the same cause** while 68 prescribed the identical lever. Three changes. **(1) One cause, one tile** — the shortfall note is not shown beside the maintenance note (the larger statement wins); applied inside the note too, where `ratioFails` and `volumeFails` were two readings of "your mileage does not climb" and stacked three consecutive volume sentences, two opening *"Your biggest week"* with different meanings. **(2) Consequence, then cause, then the one lever** — *"Peak-phase volume 52 km is 104% of week 1, below the 110% overload threshold"* became *"Your weekly mileage holds steady across this plan rather than climbing into the final weeks."* ⚠️ **Where a gap exists both figures STAY** (Hutchinson: a note that reports a constraint without naming what it costs is a disclaimer, not coaching), so the long-run shortfall gained the consequence instead of losing the numbers: *"…so the closing stretch will be new ground on the day."* Four note variants rewritten (peak-overload, lopsided 123w, structural 103w, structured-overrun 90w). **(3) Length capped, not just count** — Wood's guardrail was on COUNT and the wall arrived anyway, three items tall. **RESULT: mean 130 → 67 words, worst 254 → 117, and 510 of 513 plans carry exactly one tile (was 135/208/170 across one/two/three).** ⚠️ **The runtime word budget is NOT the guard and the code says so** — it drops whole notes and always keeps the first, so after the de-dupe it decides almost nothing; the per-note ratchet in `planRationale.test.ts` is what binds. ⚠️ **One new test was GREEN FOR THE WRONG REASON and was caught by falsifying it**: the "never shown together" assertion passed with the de-dupe removed, because the word budget was dropping the second note on length. Re-expressed on short synthetic notes so it reaches the rule it names. ⚠️ **A safety test parses this copy** — `lrFloorRounding.test.ts` reads the two figures out of the sentence because neither is stamped on `meta`; it survived only because it asserts its own fixture validity and fired loudly. Voice stays the RULE ENGINE'S: Traynor blocked routing these through the enricher for a friendlier voice, because the enricher never runs for free users and that would leave the free tier permanently with the cold copy — the registry's old optional follow-up to do so is **rejected on tier grounds**. Golden snapshots moved on `volume_constraint_note` only; parity 1,669/5,940 (28.1%), sole logic change `ratioFails && !volumeFails`; `cohort:shape` unchanged, so no plan was reclassified. verify exit 0, 2,095 tests / 228 files. |
| DOC-STATE-GATE-01 — a dated "state at end of day" paragraph can no longer go stale unnoticed | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-17 | The founder asked **three times in one day** to make sure the documents were up to date. Advisory like its siblings. Falsified before wiring: red on a real feat commit naming the SHA gap, silent on the docs commit before it; **its first catch was its own author's next commit, two minutes later.** 7 test cases both directions (`state-block-check.test.py`); the other three hook suites unaffected. ⚠️ **This row exists because the gate itself had no registry row for six hours** — `ship-record-check.py` exempts tooling-only commits, so a `feat(` that touches only `.claude/` and `CLAUDE.md` escapes it. Found by the end-of-day audit, not by a hook. |

'6-18mo'`. Of six `intermediate` + no-benchmark plans, **2 answered `6-18mo`, 2 declined the question, 2 answered `5yr+`** — so **4 of 6 land on the same VDOT and the answered and declined cases are INDISTINGUISHABLE on screen.** ⚠️ **Only 9 distinct outcomes exist for the entire no-benchmark population** (three table cells are duplicates). **(1) Precision now follows evidence.** `formatClockTimeCoarse` (in `lib/format.ts`, ADR-015's sole owner) renders the bracket estimate at minute precision: **24 min / 49 min / 1:52 / 3:54** where it read 23:46 / 49:09 / 1:51:32 / 3:54:16. Keyed on `source === 'wizard'`, the route's own structural discriminator, never inferred from confidence. The route's label lost an em dash and an overclaim (*"Rough estimate — add a benchmark…"* → *"Estimated from your wizard answers, not your running"*). ⚠️ **`/coach-preview` gains a State 4 case and a `previewData` fixture seam** — the card is PAID, auth-gated and fetches on mount, so **the majority path had never been rendered anywhere it could be looked at.** Verified on device width: action first, table one tap away, no seconds. Falsified: making `formatClockTimeCoarse` delegate to `formatClockTime` fails 4 of 6 assertions. ⚠️ **NOT FIXED HERE, routed to the Coaching Board: the bracket table itself** — twelve coaching numerics living in an API route, outside `GENERATION_CONFIG`, explained by no principle, invisible to `configPrincipleSync.test.ts` and to the coaching-guard hook. Same class as `peakKmByLevel` (§106). Also open: whether `?? '6-18mo'` should widen the estimate rather than silently pick the middle bracket, and whether `/pricing`'s claim (*"a projected finish from your real running, updated as you train"*) is deliverable on a path that has neither. verify exit 0, 2,101 tests / 229 files. |
| EST-VDOT-DERIVED-01 — the race estimate stops contradicting our own classifier (§109 Am.1) | PAID | 2026-09-17 | **Coaching Board 2026-09-17: INCORRECT on the shipped table, CORRECT for a derivation.** Convened after RACE-PROJ-PRECISION-01 fixed the presentation and the numbers underneath had still never been reviewed. ⚠️ **THE CONFLICT SCAN IS WHY THIS BOARD EXISTS: six of twelve cells asserted a VDOT that §13's own `FITNESS_VDOT_THRESHOLDS` classifies as a DIFFERENT level from the label that selected the cell.** A runner classified `experienced` was handed 45 or 48, both below `experienced_min: 50`; a `beginner` was handed 37, above `intermediate_min: 35`. Two of our own numbers disagreeing, and the one reaching runners was the one no principle explained. It also applied a bare `× 0.95` — a second conservatism doctrine against §10's ratified 3%, named nowhere — and lived in `app/api/race-times/route.ts`, **outside `GENERATION_CONFIG`, so no principle explained it, `configPrincipleSync.test.ts` could not see it and the coaching-guard hook never fired on it. The `peakKmByLevel` / §106 class exactly.** Reach, measured on the live database: **11 of 19 plans (58%) carry no benchmark**, so this table was the sole source of projected times for the majority of paying users; the whole no-benchmark population had **nine distinct outcomes**. **THE REMEDY IS DERIVATION, NOT A RE-TUNE** (chair): twelve hand-authored numbers drift from the principle beside them, which is what had already happened, so the estimate is now computed from the level's own §13 band and training age only positions the runner *within* it. Band width is `experienced_min - intermediate_min` — **§13's own two thresholds, so no new band numeric enters**; the fractions are offsets 3/7/10/13 of that band, i.e. **exactly the shipped intermediate row, so the majority path is byte-identical and the blast radius is confined to the two rows that were wrong.** Contradictions **6 of 12 → 0 of 12**, raw and discounted. ⚠️ **A DECLINED TRAINING AGE NOW WIDENS THE ESTIMATE** rather than silently taking the middle bracket: measured, 2 of 6 comparable live plans had declined and were shown figures identical to the 2 who answered. It renders as a range (`24–29 min`) with **no UI change at all**, because the card prints whatever string the route hands it. `ESTIMATE_VDOT_DISCOUNT_PCT: 5` is now named with a principle explaining why it exceeds §10's 3% (that discounts paces from a *real* benchmark). Estimator extracted to `lib/plan/estimateVdot.ts` — pure and node-testable, the `aerobicEstimate.ts` precedent. **`INV-EST-VDOT-AGREES-WITH-CLASSIFIER` is deliberately NOT a `validatePlan()` invariant** — it asserts two CONFIG SURFACES agree and no Plan is involved, so as a plan invariant it would run only when a plan happened to be generated. Total, not sampled: 12 combinations raw and discounted. **Falsified against the shipped table, where it names all six offending cells.** Willy's blocking condition verified before the ruling landed: one call site, read-only route, display-only consumer, no prescription path. Sims' provenance recorded: these are a prior of unrecorded population, Daniels' data skew male, and they are **not** sex-adjusted and must not be. Seiler vs McMillan on the curve recorded as **INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE** — do not re-tune without cohort data. verify exit 0, 2,108 tests / 230 files; **parity byte-for-byte IDENTICAL across 5,940 cases** (display-only); `configPrincipleSync` + `configConsumer` now see these keys, which they structurally could not before. |
| TT-NOTE-HONESTY-01 — the time trial stops promising free runners a paid outcome | FREE | 2026-09-17 | The §78 note told **every** runner *"Log the result in your profile and your paces update for the next block."* Two things were wrong. **(1) Recalibration is PAID** (`dynamic_reshape_r20`, ADR-014) and **free plans carry the trial too** — measured on identical input, both tiers generate `recalibration_weeks: [8]` — so a free runner was promised an outcome their tier cannot deliver, while `RecalibrationTile` one surface away already told the same runner the truth (*"part of Zonna Plus"*). **Two surfaces, one fact, disagreeing, and the honest one was not the prescription.** **(2) "In your profile" is the wrong place** — the entry point is a tile on Today; Me's route is labelled "Race benchmark". Now: *"This is a measurement, not a session. Log the time when you're done and you'll get the option to rebuild your paces around it."* True at both tiers, names no screen, and keeps ADR-014's prompted-and-confirmed model intact. ⚠️ **`INV-PLAN-COACH-NOTES-MATCH-INTENT` was an OR** (`measurement|log the result`), so dropping the instruction passed as long as the word "measurement" survived — **half the defect it was written for**. Now requires both halves. ⚠️ **Parity moved 2,508 / 5,940 (42.2%), and the cause was PROVEN rather than assumed:** reverting only this sentence returns parity to byte-for-byte IDENTICAL across all 5,940 cases, so the entire move is the string and nothing prescriptive. Golden snapshots re-baselined after confirming the only diff across five plans was that one line, 10 times. Board-exempt: coaching copy, governed by `brand.md` (ADR-017). The residual product question — should a free runner be prescribed a benchmark they cannot apply — is filed for the SLT as `TT-FREE-BENCHMARK-01` rather than answered here. |
| AUTH-BEARER-MISSING-01 — authenticated API calls always carry a bearer, guarded at build time | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | `getUserFromRequest` reads the `Authorization` header and only falls back to cookies, and `@supabase/ssr` cookie sync to the server is unreliable on native (Capacitor) — so every client call to an authenticated route must send the token explicitly. **Two sites did not, and both failed silently:** `/api/generate-plan/foundation` (the "Add Foundation Block" failure, FOUNDATION-ADD-FAIL-01's cause) and **`/api/recalibrate-zones` — a PAID feature (`dynamic_reshape_r20`, ADR-014) that 401'd every time-trial recalibration on native, which nobody had reported because nothing surfaces the 401.** Both now use `authedFetch` (the single owner of the pattern, already on 56 sites). While here, the three remaining hand-rolled inline-bearer copies were folded onto the same helper (`/api/generate-plan`, `/api/post-run-reframe`) — the one exception is `wizard-benchmark-estimate`, which wraps `getSession()` in its own `withTimeout(3500)` native-resilience guard that `authedFetch` does not provide, so it keeps a bespoke bare fetch that sends the bearer inline. **The deliverable is the guard, not the two-line fix:** `lib/supabase/authedFetchGuard.test.ts` walks `app/` + `components/`, resolves every bare `fetch('/api/…')` to its route file, and fails the build when an authenticated route (one that references `getUserFromRequest`) is called with no `Authorization` header — the `signOutOwner.test.ts` walk-the-source pattern. It also surfaces any `/api` fetch it cannot resolve to a route, so it cannot rot into a no-op. **The scan found a sixth bare-fetch site the manual audit's five-row table had missed** (`wizard-benchmark-estimate`, already correct). Falsification-tested: reverting either fix produces exactly one named offender. Non-engine change — no `lib/plan/*`, invariant or config touched; verify subset green (tsc, rules-of-hooks, 238 files / 2165 tests). |
| MARATHON-VOLUME-GATE-01 / §111 — the base-volume floor becomes a governed base-build ceiling | PAID (marathon/ultra generation) | 2026-09-18 | The engine used to refuse a marathon on `current_weekly_km < 20` from a hardcoded line in `app/api/generate-plan/route.ts` — ungoverned (no principle, invisible to `configPrincipleSync` and the coaching-guard hook), **non-monotonic** (refused 15 km/week at peak/week1 2.61, permitted 20 km/week at 2.60 — near-identical plans), offering no alternatives, and it **refused the flagship first-time charity marathoner** (persona M1, 15 km/week) at the door while every internal test passed, because the tests call `generateRulePlan` directly and the refusal lived at the route (the boundary the governed §44/§52 refusals avoid by throwing from the engine). **Coaching Board (2026-09-18) ruled CORRECT WITH AMENDMENT**, ratifying three artifacts: **principle §111** (the base-build ceiling — delivered peak ÷ the runner's raw `current_weekly_km` may not exceed the cap, for marathon/ultra), **numeric** `GENERATION_CONFIG.MAX_BASE_BUILD_RATIO = 4.0` + `BASE_BUILD_RATIO_MIN_DISTANCE_KM = 42`, and **invariant** `INV-PLAN-BASE-BUILD-RATIO` (error). The metric is peak÷current, not peak÷week1: measured, week 1 is floored at 18 km regardless of the stated base, so peak/week1 is flat ~2.6 across the low-volume cohort and cannot discriminate, while peak/current is monotonic (5 km→9.4×, 12 km→3.9×, 15 km→3.13×, 40 km→1.3×) and is the acute jump off the real base. 4.0 admits every charity persona (M1 3.13×, any cap ≤3.13 would refuse M1) and refuses the reckless case (≤10 km/week marathon, ≥4.7×). The refusal is thrown from `generateRulePlan.finalise` as `BaseVolumeError` (mirroring `PrepTimeError`/`DaysAvailableError`) with §44-style alternatives, carried by `lib/plan/baseVolume.ts` which is the single owner both the refusal and the invariant read, so they cannot drift; the invariant is the defense-in-depth backstop (the refusal pre-empts it in the generation path) and is woken by the `weekly_km x5` liveness mutation. **A reconvened board (same day) resolved a collision the first conflict-scan missed:** §111 supersedes MAINT-LABEL-01/UX-BEGINNER-01 **for the reckless tail only** (marathon `current < ~12 km`) — the honest maintenance note still fires for beginners who generate (≥12 km), `validateInputFields` still accepts a 0 base (§55), and short distances (5K/10K/HM) are outside §111. **Declared blast radius:** `verify:parity` 208/5940 (3.5%), **every one OK→REFUSED** (marathon/ultra low-base cells; every plan that still generates is byte-identical — §111 removes a set, it does not alter generated plans); sweep 14,221 plans / no new violations; `cohort:shape` byte-identical (grid's lowest volume is 20 km); `measure:fitness` personas unaffected; verify green (239 files / 2174 tests, 120 invariants, matrix 65/0, coaching HIGH 0). **The humane "not yet" screen that renders this refusal is REFUSAL-SCREEN-01; the base-building-plan alternative is SLT-deferred (Coaching Board first, `FOUNDATION_MAX_WEEKS` is 3).** The route's `longest_recent_run_km < 5` gate is the same ungoverned-number smell, a distinct coaching question, flagged for a separate sitting, not touched. |
| REFUSAL-SCREEN-01 — a plan-generation refusal reads as "not yet", not a crash | FREE | 2026-09-18 | The `GeneratePlanScreen` error view headlined every refusal **"Something went wrong building the plan."**, printed the raw engine string, and offered only **"Try again"** — so a deliberate, correct coaching refusal (§44 prep-time, §52 days, §55 input range, §111 base-volume) read as a broken app to a first-time charity runner, the bounce-off-and-never-return case. Now it keys on the **HTTP status**: a **422** is a deliberate refusal and reframes as a calm **"Not yet"** in the `CoachNoteBlock` amber palette (`--warn-bg`/`--coach-ink`/`--warn` — this is coach voice, not an error), rendering the brand-voiced message plus the levers the route returns (`base`/`prep`/`days.alternatives`) under **"What would get you there"**, with **"Adjust my answers"** instead of "Try again". A **500 or a network drop** keeps the honest "Something went wrong". No raw diagnostic string, no `--danger` alarm colour, no popup (`ui-patterns.md`). Discriminator is `res.status === 422` in the `!res.ok` handler; state `errorIsRefusal` + `errorAlternatives` drive the render. **Pairs with §111 (MARATHON-VOLUME-GATE-01)** — they deploy together or §111's refusal shows through the old crash screen. Also shipped: **COPY-DAYS-PLURAL-01** — the §52 days-gate message singularises ("1 day/week", not "1 days/week"; `daysPlural.test.ts`), and the sweep/cohort refusal-recognition regexes are plural-agnostic (grids reach only days ≥ 2, so no parity move). **Follow-up (filed under REFUSAL-SCREEN-01):** voice the pre-existing §44/§52 message internals (shouty caps, "days/wk") and add the SLT-requested weeks-remaining runway line — a parity-moving governed-message change deserving its own scope. Screen is auth+state-gated; on-device visual is the residual. Full suite green (239 files / 2174 tests), tsc + rules-of-hooks clean. |
| FOUNDATION-ADD-FAIL-01 — the foundation-add failure leaves a trace | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | The founder tapped "Add Foundation Block" and got "Couldn't add that. Try again." with **nothing recorded anywhere** — the client catch (`handleFoundationAddBlock`) set an error state with no code/message/console/ops event, and `POST /api/generate-plan/foundation` 500'd with only a server `console.error` (not a durable, queryable row). The **cause** (the call sent no `Authorization` header — `getUserFromRequest` returns null without one, and native cookies are unreliable) was fixed under AUTH-BEARER-MISSING-01 (the call now uses `authedFetch`). This is the **observability half** the backlog required first ("the real defect is that this cannot be diagnosed"): the route now records a durable `plan_foundation_add_failed` ops event on its 500 path (userId hoisted above the `try` so a throw deep in `resizeForDeferredFoundationAdd`/`composePlanWithFoundation`/`enforceViolations` can still name the runner), matching the ONBOARD-OBS-01 / STRAVA-WEBHOOK-OBS-01 pattern; and the client catch `console.error`s the failure (carrying the response status from the thrown error) instead of swallowing it, so a client-side leg (a network drop before the route) is not silent either. The engine path is proven clean (reproduced: 200, 3 weeks, 0 violations across three shapes including London 2027 first-timer low-base), so a firing here is now a real regression or environment fault. Contract `docs/contracts/api/generate-plan-foundation.md` updated. No route unit-test harness exists for this observability class; the cause is covered by `authedFetchGuard.test.ts` and the daily ops probe. **On-device re-test is the residual** (needs the founder's device). |
| ONBOARD-SKIP-LABEL-01 — the onboarding skip no longer claims it is connecting | FREE | 2026-09-18 | `ConnectRunsScreen` and `PushOnboardingScreen` (in `DashboardClient.tsx`) each ran **one `busy` boolean for two mutually exclusive actions** (connect and skip) and bound the PRIMARY button's label to it — `{busy ? 'Connecting…' : 'Connect Apple Health'}` / `{busy ? 'Setting up…' : 'Enable Notifications'}`. So tapping **"Connect later"** or **"Skip for now"** set `busy = true` and the primary button announced **"Connecting…"/"Setting up…"** — a lie told for the duration of one tap, at the exact moment a beginner is deciding whether to trust the app. The DB was always correct (skip writes `connect_runs_seen: false` / `push_permission_seen: false` and never `healthkit_connected_at`); only the label lied. Fix: the boolean became a **pending-action enum** (`'connect' | 'skip' | null` / `'enable' | 'skip' | null`) and the primary label keys on the **specific** action (`pending === 'connect' ? 'Connecting…'`), so the working label never shows during a skip; the skip link shows a neutral **"One sec…"** while its own write runs, never the connect label; the truthy `disabled`/`cursor`/`opacity` usages carry over unchanged. Guard: `lib/onboarding/onboardingSkipLabel.test.ts` walks `DashboardClient.tsx` and fails the build if a working label is bound to a bare truthy flag (the exact bug) — falsified both ways, and both screens must satisfy it (the "one owner" the backlog asked for, since the screens are internal to a 12k-line component and cannot be rendered standalone). Client UI only; full suite green (241 files / 2180 tests), tsc + rules-of-hooks clean. |
| FOUNDATION-DECIDE-LATER-01 — the foundation sheet stops promising a "later" that never comes | FREE | 2026-09-18 | The Phase-4 foundation-block sheet (`GeneratePlanScreen.tsx`, shown when the gap to `plan_start` > 28 days) offered three buttons: **Add Foundation Block** / **Start plan as-is** / **Decide later**. `handleFoundationStartNow` and `handleFoundationSkip` were **byte-for-byte identical** (both just `setFoundationAddStatus('idle'); setFoundationModalOpen(false)`), and **"Decide later" was a promise the app could not keep**: `setFoundationModalOpen(true)` appears exactly once, at generation, so nothing ever re-offered the decision — dismiss it and the choice was gone permanently (a brand-voice violation, honest being the first voice rule, as much as a UX one). SLT ruled **Fix A** (delete the button, do not build a real "later" — that needs a surface that does not exist, and inventing one is the illusion-of-progress class; the sheet-explains-the-28-weeks follow-on belongs to FIRSTRUN-MARATHON-01 touchpoint 5). Shipped: the **"Decide later" button is deleted**, and the two identical handlers are consolidated into one honestly named `handleFoundationDismiss` used by both "Start plan as-is" and the sheet's `onClose` — so the two ways out (choose "start as-is", or dismiss the sheet) are the same honest outcome (the plan starts as generated), with no hidden deferred state to lose. Removed the now-unused render-prop `close` param. Client UI only; full suite green (241 files / 2180 tests), tsc + rules-of-hooks clean. |

SLT ranked it the **best value on the backlog**: for a London 2027 first-timer it says "you are eleven weeks early" and the emotion is relief, which nobody else sells. `components/shared/RunwayRevealCard.tsx` now renders it as the **first card at the plan reveal** (`GeneratePlanScreen` preview content), **led by the number** (bold `uncovered_runway_weeks`, quiet context — "You're early / 11 weeks early"), so a long-runway first-timer reads it without scrolling. **Provenance:** rule-engine copy, so the card carries **no AIMark / CoachByline / moss AI-rail** (AI-PROVENANCE-01 — never mark hand-authored copy as AI); it is a plain metric-led card in the established card family (`--card`, tokens, `shadow-card`). **Rendered once:** deliberately not added to `planRationaleNotes`, so there is no second copy on the Plan screen — the relief lands at the reveal, and that decision is locked by a test (`planRationale.test.ts` asserts the note is not surfaced there). | FIRSTRUN-MOMENTS-01b — the first session is shown at the reveal, ahead of the wall of weeks | FREE | 2026-09-18 | A first-timer opening a 20-week marathon block sees an intimidating object and feels behind before starting. This pulls the one concrete first thing out of the wall: **"Monday. 20 min. Easy."** Wood funds it (SLT) — it lowers the activation cost of the first action, the behaviour that matters. `lib/plan/firstRun.ts → firstRunOfPlan(weeks)` (pure, node-tested) finds the **first non-rest session of the FIRST week** — a foundation week if one is prepended, which is gentler still — and returns `{ dayLabel, metric, effort }`: duration via `formatDuration` (ADR-015 — "20 min", never "20 minutes"), falling back to a rounded km distance for the rare distance-anchored session; effort mapped **honestly** (`tempo`/`quality`→Tempo, `intervals`/`hard`→Hard, else Easy) so a hard session is never called easy. Returns **null** (card absent) when there is nothing concrete to promise — no weeks, an all-rest first week, or a session with neither a duration nor a distance. `components/shared/FirstRunCard.tsx` renders it as the second reveal card, under the runway relief (01a): "you're early" then "here's where it starts". **Provenance:** rule-engine data, so **no AIMark / coach byline** (AI-PROVENANCE-01); metric-led card in the reveal card family. Gate: `lib/hardcodedUnits.test.ts` walks `app/`, `components/`, `lib/plan/`, `lib/coaching/`; 6 sites baselined with reasons (2 unreachable, 2 admin-only, 1 marketing, 1 developer-facing); a stale baseline entry and an empty scan both fail. ⚠️ Narrow by design — it cannot see a fixed label with no interpolation. |

| OPS-DBCHECK-NOISE-01 — the drift check reads the schema instead of making 21 queries fail | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | `scripts/check-db-drift.ts` answered "does this table carry `week_n`?" by SELECTing the column and reading the error, writing 21 Postgres 42703 errors and 22 HTTP 400s into the production log view on every HEALTHY run. Replaced with one call to `public.schema_columns_named(text[])` (`20260918_schema_columns_rpc.sql`, applied + ledgered; `security invoker`, read-only, execute granted to `service_role` only, revoked from anon/authenticated). ⚠️ **The noise was the smaller half:** probing "each known table" built the candidate set from `WEEK_KEYED_TABLES ∪ WEEK_KEYED_EXEMPT ∪ RLS_POLICIES` — three hand-written arrays — while the function's own header says "the authority has to be the SCHEMA", so a new table carrying `week_n` that nobody listed was invisible to the check built to find exactly that. Now schema-sourced and BIDIRECTIONAL: a listed table that has LOST its column fails too, and a stale exemption is reported. Gate: `lib/supabase/dbDriftProbe.test.ts`. |
| CI-SLOW-DRIFT-01 — test-duration drift is gated, not printed | FREE (infra) | 2026-09-18 | `vitest.config.ts` sets `slowTestThreshold: 1000` so drift is VISIBLE before it is red. It worked exactly as designed and 8.7s was printed on every green run for weeks until `targetedGrid.test.ts` went red in CI on a +1.4% change. `npm run check:slow` (`scripts/check-test-duration.ts`, in `npm run verify` after `npm run test`) reads the JSON report and applies three rules: a NEW test at or above 1.5x the reporting threshold FAILS, no test may exceed 70% of `testTimeout`, and a baselined test may not step beyond 1.4x. Baseline `lib/plan/__fixtures__/testDurationBaseline.json`, re-written with `--write` and a declared reason, like `cohort:shape`. MEASURED UNDER CONTENTION: `targetedGrid` is 15,017 ms — **50.1% of budget and 39% of the whole suite's test time**, with about one doubling of headroom left. ⚠️ **Report-only when `CI` is set**, deliberately: a CI duration compared to a dev-machine baseline is two different measurements, and CI's protection against a runaway test is `testTimeout` itself. ⚠️ Drift tolerance is loose by design; the creep is caught by the baseline being a committed number that must be re-written in a diff. |

The §52 refusal now carries the runway (*"You've got 29 weeks before race day, so the runway is fine; it's the days"*) — **only when the runway is genuinely adequate**, because asserting it to someone §44 would also refuse is a claim the data does not support. ⚠️ **The change exposed that the refusal COPY WAS AN UNDECLARED WIRE FORMAT:** eight prose matchers across five files (`cohortGrid`, `cohortShape`, `noteDurationFormat`, `targetedGrid`, `charityCohort`, `daysPlural`, `property-validate-plans`, `charity-scenarios`, `board-evidence-effort-governed`, `coaching-review-round`) identified a by-design refusal by regex over prose. All migrated to `lib/plan/designedRefusal.ts → isDesignedRefusal`, which matches the ERROR TYPE (`PrepTimeError`/`DaysAvailableError`/`BaseVolumeError`/`LongRunReadinessError`, with an `e.name` fallback for cross-worktree `instanceof`). All four test arms falsified; the audit arm needs a FUTURE `SINCE` to isolate (`./scripts/audit-docs.sh 2026-09-19`). |
| REFUSAL-ALT-REACHABLE-01 — a refusal may not offer a door that is also locked | FREE | 2026-09-18 | §113 refused a first-time marathoner whose longest run was 4 km and told them *"Or start with a half marathon plan, which asks less of your longest run."* **It asks exactly the same** — `LONG_RUN_READINESS_MIN_RACE_KM` is 21 km, so the floor governs the half identically and refuses the same runner again. Measured: at longest 4 km, marathon REFUSED, half marathon REFUSED, 10K generates, 5K generates. Now offers the 10K, which opens and builds the exact base the marathon needs. ⚠️ **Worst possible place to be wrong:** the reader is a charity first-timer told no in October for a race in April, and the charity's stated problem is that people who take a place never run — a second locked door spends their one retry. Gate: `lib/plan/refusalAlternatives.test.ts` FOLLOWS every alternative that names a distance and fails if it refuses the same runner. ⚠️ The existing `longRunReadiness.test.ts` case had **pinned the defect** (it asserted the half IS offered) and was corrected in the same commit. |
| CB-SUBFLOOR-ADMIT-01 — §113 Am.1: the floor may not manufacture the leap it then refuses over | FREE | 2026-09-18 | Coaching Board **veto** on the previous refusal. `MIN_SESSION_DISTANCE_KM.long` (5 km) was applied AFTER §45's week-1 cap, three lines later in `ruleEngine`, and won: at a 3 km longest run the cap places **3.3 km (+10%, safe)** and the floor overrode it to **5.0 km (+67%)** — then §113 refused the runner **for the leap the floor had created**. Floors now resolve per runner via `lib/plan/sessionFloors.ts → sessionFloorsFor`, bounded below by `MIN_SESSION_DISTANCE_ABSOLUTE_KM = 2`, and §113 is **runway-aware**: admitted when the runway covers `weeksToReachFloor + §44 block`, still refused otherwise (a 2 km runner with 12 weeks is refused; with 29 weeks is admitted). **MEASURED: the property sweep generates 1,216 MORE plans (13,014 → 14,230), 0 hard failures, no new violations.** ⚠️ The flat floor was MASKING two defects, both surfaced only once it moved: a §45 week-on-week breach in `applyLongRunProgressionCap` (W2 2.9 → W3 4.97, +71%) which re-floored the long run after bounding it, and a §9 long-vs-easy inversion. Four producer sites plus the validator were on the flat config; all now share the owner. `INV-PLAN-WEEK-1-2-LONG-CAP` amended — its `Math.max(rawCap, floor)` was the written permission for the defect. ⚠️ **§111's base-volume gate still refuses below ~16 km/wk** — separate rule, not ruled on. Gate: `lib/plan/subFloorAdmission.test.ts`, which exists because **no standing harness can see this cohort** (0 of 37,248 grid rows). |
| CB-BEGINNER-HILLS-01 — §28 Am.1: beginners get short hill strides | FREE | 2026-09-18 | Coaching Board ruling A **as amended on corrected evidence**. MEASURED GAP: plans containing any hills — **beginner 0.0%, intermediate 43.7%, experienced 40.5%**. Nobody decided beginners should not do hills; `hill_reps` is typed `vo2max` in the catalogue against `QUALITY_SESSIONS_PER_WEEK_MAX.beginner = 0` (§110), so hills reached them through no path at all. Every `BEGINNER_HILL_STRIDE_EVERY_N_WEEKS`-th stride run now BECOMES a hill stride run (`6×10s up a moderate gradient, walk back down`). ⚠️ **ALTERNATES, NEVER ADDS** — a hill run alongside the stride run would double the weekly neuromuscular dose; measured after: beginner stride runs 10.1 → 4.6 with hills on the remainder, **zero weeks carrying both**, intermediate/experienced unchanged at 10.1/10.2. ⚠️ **It stays a COACH NOTE ON AN EASY RUN**, which is what makes it structurally incapable of counting as quality. New owner `lib/plan/neuromuscular.ts` holds the eligibility predicate so the invariant cannot re-derive it. Parity: **1,400/5,940 changed, beginner 1400/1980, intermediate 0, experienced 0.** `cohort:shape` and `measure:fitness` UNCHANGED. ⚠️ **The strides half of the ruling was WITHDRAWN** — §28 already gave every runner strides (100%, mean 10.1); legislating it would have shipped a no-op. |
| S106-RACE-PEAK-01 — §111 was counting the marathon itself as training volume | FREE | 2026-09-18 | `deliveredPeakKm` filtered foundation weeks and `n > 0` and stopped, so for a marathon the **42.2 km RACE** landed in §111's numerator. Measured on a beginner marathon: the training peak is **week 16 at 52 km** — exactly `PEAK_KM_BY_LEVEL.MARATHON.beginner` — while week 20 (race) reports **59 km**. §111 scored every marathon runner's build **13% higher than the training they actually do**, demanding a 14.75 km/week base instead of 13. **Effect on the door: a beginner with a 29-week runway is admitted from 12 km/week instead of 16; the property sweep generates 240 MORE plans (14,230 → 14,470).** ⚠️ DEFECT FIX, not doctrine — §111's header defines the metric as "the acute stimulus" and ratified it citing "5 km/week → 47 km peak", a figure that already excluded the race; the code never matched the principle. ⚠️ **Neither plan grid can see this** (minimum `current_weekly_km` is 20), so `cohort:shape` and `measure:fitness` report no change. Gate: `lib/plan/racePeakExclusion.test.ts`. |
